                        | Worker claim path for the inbox + consumers that re-verify with GitHub before acting (P3-3/P3-4); reconcile job that redelivers failed deliveries via the GitHub API (a 503 is not retried by GitHub); rate limit on the webhook route (P7-3); schedule `runRetention` (now incl. the inbox) in the worker; `NODE_ENV=production` in deployment docs (Phase 10); once P3-3 adds a processed marker, the inbox purge must skip unprocessed rows; events not decoded yet: `pull_request.edited` (base change), `pull_request.ready_for_review`/`converted_to_draft`, `installation.suspend`/`unsuspend`, `installation_repositories`. Review round 1 applied: one pool with timeouts and an error listener, 5 s inbox guard → 503, 25 MiB limit, installer (`sender`) recorded, `FLY_APP_NAME` counts as production, unsupported account types ignored with a reason. Review round 2 applied: `sender` nullable and `[bot]`-tolerant (an uninstall is never refused over its actor), header precheck before the body is read, 64 MiB in-flight budget, 30 s request timeout enforced at the HTTP server (Fastify's option alone is not, Node needs `headersTimeout` ≤ `requestTimeout` at construction), insert-only 3 s timeout and 6 s guard, idempotent shutdown; P3-3 must handle `sender: null`. Review round 3 applied: inbox insert bounded server-side (`SET LOCAL` lock 2 s / statement 3 s, no orphaned backends), in-flight budget counts bytes actually received. Final review: every statement of the inbox transaction carries the client-side timeout; a declared length over the body limit gets 413, not 503. Watch: one unreproduced full-suite failure (likely a testcontainers start-up timeout under load)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| P3-3  | review | Install → org, uninstall → deletion after the 7-day grace, reinstall → cancel (ADR 0009, threat model L1–L29). Migration 0007: roles `rocky_worker` (only it creates orgs, claims inbox rows, runs `retention.delete_org`) and `rocky_lifecycle` (owns the `worker.*` SECURITY DEFINER functions); `orgs.account_id` (unique per forge, immutable via trigger) + `installer_forge_user_id` (candidate only); inbox lease/attempts/outcome/parked/last_error with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim, completion inside the tenant transaction, 5 attempts then parked; purge skips unprocessed rows; `retention.orgs_due_for_deletion`; `delete_org` also removes the org's finished inbox rows and writes a tombstone to `app.org_deletions`. GitHub re-verification `GET /app/installations/{id}` with an RS256 app JWT from `node:crypto` (`packages/forge-github/src/{app-auth,installations}.ts`; fixtures in `packages/forge-github/fixtures/` follow GitHub's documented response shapes with synthetic ids and are not recorded from GitHub, and webhook payloads are built in `packages/forge-github/src/testing.ts`); worker config validates `GITHUB_APP_ID`/`GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (RSA ≥ 2048), fail closed. Worker jobs: inbox drain, deletion sweep, `runRetention`. Erasure extension points `OrgErasers` (`object_storage`, `embeddings`) in `packages/core/src/erasure.ts`. Tests: `apps/worker/src/lifecycle.e2e.test.ts` (signed webhook → inbox → claim → GitHub fixtures → org; uninstall → 7 days → sweep → every org-id column empty; reinstall cancels; forged/planted rows, deleted-while-installed, outage, concurrency, crash, poison, isolation, logs), `packages/db/src/{inbox,deletion,isolation,roles}.test.ts`, `packages/forge-github/src/{app-auth,installations}.test.ts`, `apps/worker/src/{config,jobs}.test.ts`. Review round 1 applied (threat model L1–L24): the server's `orgs` UPDATE narrowed to settings/keys by column grants, `delete_after`/installer/identity worker-only (trigger too), deadline bounded to [now+grace, now+grace+1 h], never infinity, out-of-window deadlines rescheduled (H1); only GitHub's JSON 404 is "gone", `GET /app` app check gates claims and sweeps (M2); claims per forge (M3); contradictions retried 3 attempts as strikes (S1); the sweep re-verifies with GitHub, cancels with an audit if the installation exists, defers on errors (S1c); one live lease per installation, oldest first (S2); transient failures back off to 1 h and park only at 72 h, strikes park at 5 (S3); lease = batch × worst case, short-lease rows released unstarted (S4); `erasing_at` marks the point of no return, a reinstall then gets a fresh org, erasure resumable (S6); `delete_org` deletes the erased installation's pending non-installation rows and scrubs sender/login on the rest (L4); key removed from `process.env` after parsing (L6); every FK holder asserted non-empty before erasure, non-public tables keyed by installation/account pinned (N1); Enterprise accounts → `unsupported_account_type` with a documentation-shaped fixture (N2); request headers asserted (N3); `pnpm seed` hint for refused logins (N4); grants/policies/function bodies snapshotted across one-step down/up (N5); uninstall-before-install (N6). Each control mutation-checked: reverting it fails its test. Review round 2 applied (L1–L26): claim-time superseding removed, since a planted newer row could swallow a genuine uninstall (A); CHECK `webhook_deliveries_installation_shape` mirrors `minimalEvent` for installation rows (A); `delete_org` deletes rows of an unexpected shape and guards its scrub, so a malformed pending row cannot wedge an erasure (B); `delete_org` matches by the erased installation, and by account only for finished rows of installations no live org holds, leaving a reinstall's rows and installer alone (C); contradiction window counted in strikes (SF2); lease budget on the worker's clock from before the claim, lease `(batch + 1) × budget`, released rows delayed, no immediate re-loop (SF3); trigger fence tested on its own (N-a); sweep forge filter tested (N-b). Mutation-checked again. Review round 3 applied (L1–L27): the claim's cost no longer grows with the backlog: a ready queue `app.webhook_queue` maintained by statement-level triggers, at most `3 × limit + 10` candidates per lane, head and lease probes on partial indexes (`webhook_deliveries_pending_idx`, `webhook_deliveries_leased_idx`); claims run under `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` (5 s); leases from `clock_timestamp()` (`leased_at`); a lease holder's verified completion collapses earlier pending, unleased same-type rows of its installation (`superseded`), never on claim, failure or park; `installationId` must be a JSON number (CHECK); the inbox job's re-loop rule tested in `jobs.test.ts`; residual risks for per-installation delay and older-installation pending rows documented. Mutation-checked again. Review round 4 applied (L1–L29): the server refuses to start when its login is a member of `rocky_worker` (worker URL or superuser; `WorkerLoginError`, exit 1, no credential in the message) and its pool checks every new connection the same way (L28, `apps/server/src/server.db.test.ts`); `retention.delete_org` refuses an org not marked erasing (L29, `deletion.test.ts`; SQLSTATE and wording corrected in round 5); fixture wording made honest; ADR 0009 residual risks corrected (a 404 for `installation.deleted` is acted on at once, bounded by the grace and the sweep's re-check) and extended. Review round 5 applied (L28, L29): the server's login check is now an allow-list (`checkServerLogin` in `packages/db/src/roles.ts`, `ServerLoginError` replaces `WorkerLoginError`): for both `session_user` and `current_user`, no privilege attribute, no membership other than `rocky_app` (any grant option), not a member of the database's or `public.orgs`' owner, and `pg_has_role(..., 'MEMBER')` false for `rocky_worker`, `rocky_lifecycle`, `rocky_retention`; at boot and in the pool's `onConnect`, failing closed on errors and unexpected answers; the migrator/owner URL (B1), a SET-only `rocky_worker` grant (S1), `options=-c role=rocky_app` on the worker URL (S2), a `rocky_lifecycle` member (S3) and a `BYPASSRLS` login are refused (`apps/server/src/server.db.test.ts`, fixtures shown non-vacuous); the hook covers new connections only, and `maxLifetimeSeconds` 300 bounds a grant made while connections are open (S4, documented as a residual risk); `delete_org` raises `55000` for "not erasing" and the sweep counts it as a failure, not a skip (N2, `deletion.test.ts`, `lifecycle.e2e.test.ts`); 0007 and `packages/db/THREATS.md` no longer claim the database forces the re-check and erasers: it guarantees only that `erasing_at` is set first, by the worker; the sweep enforces the rest in code (N1); the `onConnect`-exists assertion replaced by fail-closed unit tests of the hook (N3). Mutation-checked. Review round 6 applied (L28): `checkServerLogin` now collects, for `session_user` and `current_user`, every role the login reaches by any path and grant option (`pg_has_role(..., 'MEMBER')`) and requires that set to be exactly `rocky_app`, with the privilege attributes ORed across the login and the whole set, so grants to `rocky_app` itself (`pg_write_all_data`, a `BYPASSRLS` role) and attributes set on it are refused (S-1); refuses when the login or any reached role owns the database, `public.orgs` or any object in the database (`pg_shdepend`, deptype `o`) (S-2); qualifies every catalog object, function and type with `pg_catalog.` (N-1, a shadowing `search_path` test); refuses ADMIN OPTION (`admin_option`, N-2); all in `packages/db/src/roles.test.ts` "checkServerLogin (review round 6)", each red before the fix. Documented as residual risks in ADR 0009: a long checkout is never recycled (N-3), a reachable but unmigrated database makes the server exit at boot (`check_failed`, `42P01`) (N-4), a check slower than `connectionTimeoutMillis` at boot boots the server as "unreachable" while the hook still refuses every connection (N-5). Status of the acceptance criteria: criterion 1 is met with documentation-shaped fixtures pending real recording (needs a human with a test installation of the app); criterion 2 is proven for every Postgres table, with the R2 (`object_storage`) and embeddings-store erasers as placeholders until P5/P8. Dev upgrade from `main`: add `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` (see `.env.example`) to `.env`, then `pnpm seed` (migrates and creates `rocky_worker_dev`); a dev database that ran an earlier version of this branch's 0007 must drop its volume (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml down -v`), because `down 1` fails on the ledger checksum | P5 must replace the `object_storage` eraser (R2 prefix) and P8 the `embeddings` eraser if vectors leave Postgres (`apps/worker/src/main.ts` slots: the type forces each slot to exist, but the `nothingStoredYet` placeholder can stay); P5: refuse to start when R2 is configured while `object_storage` is still the placeholder; P5: writers refuse orgs with `erasing_at` set and/or the erasers run again after `delete_org` (driven by `app.org_deletions`), otherwise a write landing between the external erase and `delete_org` is orphaned; P3-4 consumes `pull_request.*` inbox rows and handles suspended installations; the inbox purge skips unprocessed rows, so `pull_request.*` rows pile up until P3-4: schedule P3-4 next, or purge pending `pull_request.*` rows older than the retention period; P3-4: the claim skips a lane head whose type is not in `p_event_types`, so storing a future `installation.*` type before the worker handles it stops that installation's lane; P7-1 turns `installer_forge_user_id` into a first admin only after verifying it on GitHub, and owns user rows; runbook for parked inbox rows (inspect `last_error` and `strikes`, requeue or delete as the migrator) and an operator view; L-1: parked rows are kept forever with the sender and account login: scrub them on park or purge parked rows after a longer period (with the runbook); L-1: the deletion sweep's due list is capped at 20 and ordered by `delete_after`, so 20 orgs whose eraser keeps failing hold back later ones: deprioritise repeated failures or raise the cap; inbox-depth, oldest-pending-age (per installation) and parked-row alerting; P3-4: a periodic installation reconciler: webhooks lost while the server is down are not redelivered by GitHub, and an uninstall can be believed away when GitHub's API still answers 200 within the contradiction window; it lists `GET /app/installations` or re-checks each live org (and any scheduled deadline) and schedules deletion on GitHub's JSON 404; also consider a longer contradiction window for `deleted` only; P3-3 criterion (needs a human with a test installation of the app): re-record and sanitise real `GET /app`, `GET /app/installations/{id}` and `installation.created`/`deleted` payloads to replace the documentation-shaped fixtures and the built webhook payloads; `scripts/security/semgrep.sh` scans the main checkout's tracked file list when run inside a git worktree (it must use the worktree's own `git ls-files`); decode `installation.suspend`/`unsuspend` if the product needs to show it; deployment docs (Phase 10): a second database login for the worker (`WORKER_DATABASE_URL`) and the app credentials in its environment; existing dev `.env` files need the new `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` line, then `pnpm seed` (see the upgrade note in ADR 0009, Consequences); store the webhook's `installation.app_id` and park on a mismatch with `GITHUB_APP_ID` (the reader's answer is already checked; N-d)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| P3-4  | review | PR mirror (ADR 0010, threat model M1–M10). The worker drains the `pull_request` inbox lane (job `pull_requests`, batches of 5, after the app check): each row re-verifies the installation (`GET /app/installations/{id}`; gone → `installation_not_found`, suspended → `installation_suspended`, no token minted), waits for the org (`org_not_ready` strike, then `no_org`), opens a repository session (`createGitHubForge`, `packages/forge-github/src/repositories.ts`): an installation token minted per row with `repository_ids: [repo]` and `contents`/`pull_requests: read` only, the answer checked (one repository, read-only) or revoked and refused, revoked on close, never logged or stored; re-reads the pull request (422 → `repository_inaccessible`, 404 → `pull_request_not_found`) and, once per head, the revision diff from `compare/{base}...{head}` up to 1 MiB (`max_bytes`, cut at a line), GitHub's 406 `too_large` (`forge_limit`) or missing commits (`unavailable`); one `withOrg` transaction upserts `repos`, `pull_requests` (never over a newer `forge_updated_at`) and `pr_revisions` (diff bytes + sha256 + `diff_truncation`; the diff text is not stored) and completes the inbox row. Migration 0008: `pull_requests.forge_updated_at`, `pr_revisions.diff_bytes/diff_sha256/diff_truncation` with CHECKs tying them to `diff_truncated`; no grant changes. `Forge` port: `openRepository`/`RepositorySession`, `locateRepository`; `ForgeReadFailure` `installation_suspended`. Rebuild: `pnpm mirror:rebuild <owner/name> [--limit N]` (`apps/worker/src/{mirror-rebuild,rebuild-mirror}.ts`) lists every pull request (newest created first, up to the limit), skips the ones already current, rewrites the rest through the webhook path, deletes nothing (`notListed` counted). P3-3 follow-ups closed: pending `pull_request.*` rows are consumed; claims must cover whole lanes (`laneEventTypes`, `claimSql`) and the inbox CHECK is pinned to `FORGE_EVENT_TYPES`, so no lane stalls behind an unclaimed type; an unhandled type is parked (`unhandled_event_type`). Criterion: `apps/worker/src/mirror.e2e.test.ts` "webhooks build the mirror; after it is lost, the rebuild recreates exactly that state" (opened, synchronize, merged, closed+reopened, `forge_limit` and `max_bytes` diffs; repository rows deleted; rebuild; state equal field by field; a second run reads only the listing), plus drift repair, limit, refusals, replay, planted rows, suspension, org wait, outage, tenant separation, log and `pg_dump` hygiene. Other tests: `packages/forge-github/src/repositories.test.ts`, `packages/db/src/mirror.test.ts`, `packages/db/src/inbox.test.ts` "P3-4: claims whole lanes only…", `apps/worker/src/{jobs,mirror-rebuild}.test.ts`. Fixtures are documentation-shaped with synthetic ids, not recorded (`packages/forge-github/fixtures/README.md`). Review round 1 applied (M11–M13): for `opened`/`synchronize` a GitHub head that differs from the event's is a strike (`forge_head_mismatch`) before it is believed; a compare 404/422 is a strike (`forge_diff_unavailable`) before it is recorded `unavailable`, and an `unavailable` reading is not counted as read and is replaced by a real one (`recordRevision`, `headDiffRead`), so later events and the rebuild repair it; `ForgeDiff` carries the raw bytes and their sha256 (no decode before hashing; `diff_bytes` never exceeds the cap); explicit `org_id` filters next to RLS in `mirror.ts`; runbook covers the rate limit and `--limit`. | P3-6  | review | Repository access check (ADR 0011, threat model A1–A14): a member sees a repository's data only while GitHub says they can read it; Rocky membership alone is not enough. `packages/core/src/access.ts` `createRepositoryAccessCheck`: cache keyed per validated org + member + repository; allow cached ≤ 5 min counted from before the question, never extended (an outage after expiry denies), a late allow is a deny; denies 30 s (definitive) / 5 s (transient); bounded LRU (`maxEntries`, default 10 000); one in-flight question per key; `invalidateMember`/`invalidateRepository`/`invalidateOrg`/`clear` with a generation guard; every throw or unexpected answer denies. Port `UserAccessReader` (`packages/core/src/forge.ts`); GitHub adapter `createGitHubUserAccessReader` (`packages/forge-github/src/user-access.ts`): `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` with the user's token, `permissions.pull` and the same repository id, 404 → `not_found`, 401 → `token_rejected`, 403 → `forbidden`, everything else throws; `loadRepositoryAccessContext` (`packages/db/src/access.ts`, inside `withOrg`); composition `createRepositoryAccess`/`memberAccessSource` (`apps/server/src/repository-access.ts`): token decrypted per GitHub question (purpose `github_token`, record id = member id), keyring destroyed, ids-only decision log. No routes mounted, no migration, no new grants. | P7-1  | review | GitHub login, sessions and CSRF (ADR 0020, threat model L1–L19; review round 1 addressed: only active org members or a User account's owner sign in, durable `removed_at`, touch/idle guards in SQL and a trigger, auth-scope error handler, token expiry required in production). GitHub App user authorization with PKCE S256: single-use state (sha256 in `app.login_attempts`, consumed by `DELETE … RETURNING`) bound to the browser by an HMAC'd `__Host-rocky_login` cookie; server-side code exchange (`packages/forge-github/src/oauth.ts`); identity by numeric `GET /user` id; orgs from `GET /user/installations` mapped through `auth.login_orgs` (SECURITY DEFINER, owner `rocky_auth`, six `orgs` columns). Migration 0013: `app.users`, `app.sessions` (sha256 of a 256-bit token; idle 8 h, absolute ≤ 24 h and ≤ the GitHub token; column grants so the app cannot extend, move or revive a session), `app.session_orgs` (cascades from `members`), `members.forge_access_expires_at`. Token stored envelope-encrypted per org (purpose `github_token`, record id = member), refresh token never stored, cleared at the last logout. Cookies `__Host-`, Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Path=/ (dev-only exception: http on loopback). Global CSRF guard (Sec-Fetch-Site, exact Origin/Referer, session-bound synchronizer token; webhooks the declared exemption). Acceptance: `apps/server/src/auth/auth.db.test.ts` "session fixation (acceptance criterion)", "CSRF (acceptance criterion)", "open redirect (acceptance criterion)"; plus `return-to.test.ts`, `csrf.test.ts`, `config.test.ts`, `packages/db/src/sessions.test.ts`, `packages/forge-github/src/oauth.test.ts`; isolation and deletion suites extended. Web: `apps/web/src/session.ts`. | P7-3  | review | Security headers, CSP and rate limits (ADR 0025, threat model H1–H27). Review round 1 applied: malformed URLs (`GET /%`) get the headers and a fixed 400 (`frameworkErrors`), a root error handler with fixed bodies, `webhook.ip` spent only by rejections and split by the own-App claim, `webhook.installation` spent only by queued deliveries, per-scope rate-limit stores, logout and org switching counted per CSRF-proven session, `ROCKY_TRUST_PROXY=none` refused on Fly with the mode logged at boot and a rate-limited fallback warning, stricter SPA fallback paths, `check-report.mjs` tested. One root `onSend` hook (`apps/server/src/security/headers.ts`) on every answer: CSP `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'` for documents and `default-src 'none'` for the rest, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, `Permissions-Policy`, COOP/CORP/COEP, `no-store` unless a static file chose, HSTS in production; the auth and setup pages keep their stricter `pageCsp` (no duplicates: `headers.test.ts`). The server serves the Vite build from `ROCKY_WEB_DIR` (route table built at boot, symlinks and inline content refused: `web-app.test.ts`); `apps/web/src/build.test.ts` builds the app in `pnpm verify` and finds no inline script or style. Fixed 404 body. Rate limits (`security/rate-limit.ts`, `route-limits.ts`): `auth.login`/`auth.callback`/`auth.logout` 30/10 min and `auth.session` 300/min per client address through `AuthDeps.rateLimit` (a limited login writes no `login_attempts` row: `auth/rate-limit.test.ts`); `webhook.ip` 600/min before the body and `webhook.installation` 600/min after the signature (forged deliveries spend nothing: `webhooks.rate-limit.test.ts`); `ask` defined for P9 (`app.rateLimits`); 429 + `Retry-After` + `{"error":"rate_limited"}`; keys HMAC'd before store and log; `ROCKY_RATE_LIMIT_<SCOPE>` overrides, malformed refused. `ROCKY_TRUST_PROXY` none/fly/xff:<hops>, required in production, fly only on Fly.io, IPv6 per /64 (`client-ip.test.ts`). **Acceptance:** `pnpm security:zap` (pinned ZAP 2.17.0 baseline against the real server, built SPA and a throwaway Postgres; also a CI job): High 0, Medium 0; accepted with reasons in `scripts/security/zap/rules.tsv`: Low 10096 (login cookie expiry) and Informational 10049, 10109, 10112; negative control without the hook: Medium 10020 and 10038, run fails. Rebased onto P7-2 (ADR 0024): the web app's file routes and `/` declare `publicRoute` and have rows in the route-permission matrix (with header assertions on every cell, denials included). CodeQL round: `findInlineContent` and `build.test.ts` parse with parse5 (pinned) instead of a script-tag regexp (js/bad-tag-filter), and `loadWebApp` reads each file through one `O_NOFOLLOW` handle it `fstat`s (js/file-system-race); threat model H28–H30. | H4-4  | review | Structured-output schema fix (follow-up bug, not a plan row; ADR 0013 amendment H4-4, threat model S1–S6). Every real analysis failed as `internal_error` at the graph call before a request: the Anthropic SDK's `transformJSONSchema` refuses the graph schema's `.meta()` "at least one of" constraints (a combinator beside a `type` with typeless variants: `Payload`, `ViewScope`/`StepFocus` selection). Behind it, an offline probe (`max_tokens: 1`, 400s unbilled) found the API refuses the graph twice more: recursive `View`/`JsonValue` definitions, then the grammar limits (24 optional parameters; "compiled grammar is too large" for lanes + nodes + edges + flows + views + walkthrough together). Fix: `providerJsonSchema` (`packages/core/src/analysis/provider-schema.ts`) folds a type-sided combinator into the description for every role's sent schema; `LlmOutputSchema.mode` (`structured` \| `prompt`): the graph is `prompt` (schema after the instructions in the cached system block, `FINAL_INSTRUCTION_PROMPT_SCHEMA`, no `output_config.format`; `unfenced` strips one Markdown fence before parsing); the validator is unchanged either way. `wireSchema` maps a refused schema to `LlmCallError("invalid_request", { origin: "sdk_schema_transform" })`; `LlmCallError.origin` (regex-checked tag) and `AnalysisOutcome.diagnostic` (`origin`, `errorClass` = regex-checked class name or `unknown`) reach the worker log (`errorOrigin`, `errorClass`; the "analysis failed"/"analysis requeued" lines now log `errorCode`, since `code` was being redacted) and the CLI (`analysis failed: <code> (<origin>, <class>)`). `PIPELINE_VERSION` p4-4.2. Tests: `wire-schema.test.ts` (the real SDK transform over every role via `STAGE_CALLS`, no open object, no type beside a combinator, no `$defs` cycle for enforced roles; fails on the previous main), `provider-schema.test.ts`, `output.test.ts`, contract test "prompt-mode output" on all four platforms, `llm.test.ts` origin shapes, `pipeline.test.ts` diagnostics (hostile names, primitives, trapping proxy), `analysis.e2e.test.ts` and `cli/analyze.test.ts` log/stderr checks. Evidence: a real CLI run (`pnpm -s rocky analyze … --max-tokens 200000`) on a private dogfooding pull request completed (one call per role; triage 3,271 + review 2,186 + graph 9,877 = 15,334 tokens; the graph's cache write of 8,229 is the schema in the system block). | H4-5  | review | The change map shows logic only (follow-up, not a plan row; ADR 0014 amendment H4-5 with threat model A22–A26, ADR 0013 and ADR 0021 amendments H4-5). `packages/core/src/analysis/map-scope.ts`: `mapCategory` (path only: `test`, `ci`, kept runtime definitions, `tooling` by name, `docs`, `tooling` by directory, else `logic`; rule table in the ADR) and `changeMapScope` (the logic files, or the tests of a test-only PR, or nothing). The pipeline sends the graph role only the mapped files (their paths and diffs; the input budget counts them alone), records `provenance.graphUnavailable.reason: "no-logic"` (schema 1.2.0, `BriefingUnavailableReason` split off) with no call when nothing is mapped, and refuses a card or edge backed by a file outside the set (`checkGraphFiles`: `nodes[i].files[j].path: UNKNOWN_FILE`, repair round, then `analysis-failed`). The graph asks for lanes, nodes and edges only (`GraphAnswer = Graph.pick`) and is an enforced output again (`GRAPH_OUTPUT` structured; prompt mode kept, unused); one line in `GRAPH_INSTRUCTIONS` about config that feeds code; `PIPELINE_VERSION` unchanged (units did not change, H4-4 N4). Renderer and web print a fixed sentence for `no-logic` (about the path rules, not the code); the CLI `--dry-run` plan applies the scope and says why the graph call is skipped. Review round 1 (B1, S1–S7, one security low) addressed: anchored rules, scope over every non-noise file, stray references stripped not refused, documents stamped with the lowest minor they need (`requiredSchemaVersion`). Round 2: a richer graph prompt adapted from PR Lens (components by role, unchanged context nodes, lanes as the reader's model, hero edges, specific edge labels, badges, a worked example of Rocky's own; 925 → 3,135 characters) and line ranges on file references, validated against the file's new-side hunks and stripped when outside (`stats.graphRangesStripped`); a ranged card opens its file at that line. Tests: `map-scope.test.ts` (205 cases incl. a fast-check property), `validate.test.ts`, `pipeline.test.ts` H4-5 (logic only, docs-only, test-only, outside-set cards, strict answer, budget), `wire-schema.test.ts` (every role enforced, `$defs` cycles, ≤ 24 optional fields), `v1-2.test.ts`, `render.test.ts`, `briefing-model.test.ts`, `pr-847.test.ts`. Evidence: real runs on the private dogfooding PR (29 files) before and after: graph call input 37,994 → 11,029 tokens (total 48,294 → 16,384), cards 10 (5 logic, 1 tests, 3 config, 1 docs) → 5 (the billing modules, the reconcile function, the package index and the memory store; a first run also drew `.gitleaks.toml`, which made scanner configuration tooling), all backed by `billing/src/*.ts`; `pnpm evals run --fake --injection` gate passed against the baseline (recall and precision unchanged, injection totals unchanged). | H4-13 | review | A click in the diff no longer scrolls (dogfooding fix, not a plan row). Cause: the `.diff` list is focusable, so a click on code, blank row space, a hunk header or a flag focused it, and its focus handler (meant for a keyboard Tab-in) moved focus to the first line of the rendered window, which includes the overscan above the viewport, and revealed it: a scroll up, and a dropped text selection. `DiffBody` now runs that redirect only when the list matches `:focus-visible` (keyboard focus); a Tab-in lands on the first line whose top is on screen below the sticky file header (`firstOnScreen` in `use-virtual-list.ts`, `firstFrom` in `windowing.ts`), so it no longer scrolls either. Clicking a line number was already still (its focus target is the button) and is unchanged. Fix round: a click on code, a row's blank space, a hunk header or a flag makes the nearest new-side line active (`setActive` only: no focus, no scroll, selection kept; buttons, links, text boxes and cards are skipped), so Tab after a click focuses the clicked line in place instead of the pinned old one; ArrowUp/Down, PageUp/Down, Home and End also work from the list itself after a click; `firstOnScreen` rounds up; a Tab-in with no line on screen falls back to the last rendered line, never the overscan above. E2E: "after a click on code, Tab focuses the clicked line in place", "after a click on code, ArrowDown moves from the clicked line". Tests: `windowing.test.ts` "firstFrom", `diff.e2e.spec.ts` "pointer clicks keep the scroll (H4-13)" (code, row blank space, hunk header, flag; after an arrow-key move; drag selection; line number opens the composer; Tab-in on screen). No baselines changed. | H4-8  | review | The briefing is a short TL;DR (dogfooding follow-up, not a plan row; ADR 0014 amendment H4-8, schema 1.4 in `docs/schema/analysis-document.md`). Schema: `briefing.tldr` (`Tldr`, `TldrItem`), 2–4 one-line prose bullets of at most 160 characters; `briefing.summary` optional, a briefing needs one of the two (refine + `anyOf` in the JSON Schema); `BriefingFields` exported for projections; `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1.4.0, stamped only on a document with a TL;DR (`requiredSchemaVersion` now also takes the briefing; `MAP_SECTIONS_SCHEMA_VERSION` is 1.3.0); the document's verdict reason keeps 500 characters for older documents. Pipeline: `BriefingAnswer` asks for `tldr` (required) and a verdict reason of at most `MAX_VERDICT_REASON_ANSWER_LENGTH` (200); `BRIEFING_INSTRUCTIONS` asks for a TL;DR led by what the PR does, then the riskiest thing, the key finding by its id and where to start, no file or tool lists, at most four sentences, and a one-sentence reason; the AI briefing is stored without a summary; Rocky's fallback briefing keeps the fixed summary. UI: the verdict, then a "TL;DR" heading over a bulleted list (`briefingLead`, `.tldr-list`, cyan markers, `--text` text); documents without a TL;DR show the summary paragraph as before (fixture PR 858). CLI `render.ts` prints a TL;DR block or the summary. pr-847 fixture is now a 1.4 document. Evals fakes, adversary and the fake baseline updated (scores and injection totals unchanged). | H4-7  | review | The map's flows and walkthrough as their own model calls (dogfooding follow-up, not a plan row; ADR 0014 amendment H4-7 with threat model A27–A35, ADR 0013/0021 notes, schema 1.3 in `docs/schema/analysis-document.md`). After the graph call succeeds, two more calls on the graph role, each an enforced output: `FLOWS_OUTPUT` (the document's `Flow` without the message `payload`, from the new `FlowMessageBase`; 9 optional fields, no `$defs`) and `WALKTHROUGH_OUTPUT` (three to seven steps of id, heading, body and a flat node/edge focus; 2 optional fields), both through the SDK transform in `wire-schema.test.ts`. Input is the graph call's parts (title, mapped files) plus the map's outline (`graph`) and, for the walkthrough, the flows' outline (`flows`), all untrusted parts; instructions `FLOWS_INSTRUCTIONS`/`WALKTHROUGH_INSTRUCTIONS` (PR Lens guidance paraphrased, credited in `NOTICE`) in `PROMPT_TEXTS`. `stripFlowRefs`/`stripWalkthroughRefs` drop participants, messages, flows, focus ids and steps the map does not declare (file refs and ranges by the graph's rules), counted in `stats.flowsStripped`/`walkthroughStripped`; the rest is checked in the whole document (`partitionIssues` routes `graph.flows`/`graph.walkthrough` issues to their call), one repair round, then the section is absent with `provenance.flowsUnavailable`/`walkthroughUnavailable` (`analysis-failed`, `too-large`, `timeout`; schema 1.3, stamped only when used; `applyKnownFields` owns both) and the same code in `stats.flows`/`walkthrough` (`skipped` when there was no map: `no-logic`, too large, graph failed). Metering and the CLI cap apply unchanged; the estimate counts the mapped diffs once per graph-role call and the map's stages only with a map; `stats.usageByStage` per stage (redactor-safe names); repair issues are sent back under the model's own indices; `--dry-run` plans both as "if the map is drawn". Scripted fakes answer by stage (`stageOf` in `@rocky-reviews/core/testing`); the injection adversary attacks the two stages; the fake evals baseline regenerated with a 2M cap (279 graph-role calls, 980K tokens; scores and injection totals unchanged). Tests: `pipeline.test.ts` H4-7 (happy path, input parts, fenced injection text, each call failing independently incl. a thrown retryable, stripping, a stripped flow still repaired, the no-map skip, the leak guard on both sections, token counts), `validate.test.ts`, `v1-3.test.ts`, `known-fields.test.ts`, `property.test.ts`, `wire-schema.test.ts`, `stages.test.ts`, `pr-847.test.ts`. | H4-15 | review | The window no longer scrolls when a column reaches its end (dogfooding bug, not a plan row). Cause: `.sr-only` text (`position: absolute`) inside the scrolling columns had the initial containing block as its containing block (no positioned ancestor), so it escaped `.col { overflow: auto }` and its static position deep in the scrolled diff stretched the document (scrollHeight 1278 > 900 at 1440 px). Fix in `apps/web/src/styles/shell.css`: `.col` and `.panel-body` are `position: relative`, so absolutely positioned descendants scroll and clip with their column; the drawers' `position: fixed` (`.sidebar[data-open]`, `.panel[data-open]`, more specific and later) still wins. Tests: `apps/web/e2e/shell.e2e.spec.ts` "page overflow" (scroll `#main`, `#sidebar` and the panel body to the end, then `scrollHeight <= innerHeight` and `scrollY === 0` after `scrollTo`) at 1440, 1024 (panel drawer closed and open) and 375 (drawers closed, Files open, panel open); all four failed before the fix. | H4-16 | review | The diff is preloaded with the briefing (dogfooding fix, not a plan row; ADR 0034 §5 "Preload (H4-16)"). The first file opened waited for a cold diff read from GitHub (about 2.6 s); later files were fast. The real PR page now starts the one diff load when the briefing is shown (`Shell` calls `useDiff().preload` when `preloadsDiff`: an analysis for the PR's head that lists a file to open), through `DiffProvider`'s `preload` prop (server source only; fixtures and a signed-out page never preload). Loading rules moved to `diff/diff-loader.ts`: one load per page, shared; a preload failure shows nothing on the briefing; a passing one (`forge_failed`, `rate_limited`, `failed`) is tried once more when a file opens, a lasting one is shown then; a load a file view waited on is never retried. Opening a PR now spends one `diff` read even if no file is opened (documented in ADR 0034). Tests: `diff-loader.test.ts`, `real-pr.e2e.spec.ts` "H4-16: …" (no second request and no Loading state on the first file; a failed preload retried on open; no preload on an older push). | H4-17 | review | Copied diff code is only code (dogfooding fix, not a plan row). Cause: the screen-reader sign word ("added: ", "removed: ") sat inside `code.src`, so a selection across lines copied it. The flag's " (AI)" is `sr-only no-copy` (`.no-copy` in `shell.css`). `.ln .no`, `.ln .sign` and `.no-copy` also set `-webkit-user-select: none`: WebKit ignores the unprefixed property, so Safari copied line numbers and signs too. Checked in Chromium (selection and clipboard), WebKit and Firefox (selection; a one-off run with Playwright's WebKit and Firefox). Flags' visible text ("✓ Checked" and the note) is still copied when a selection spans it. Rule (`packages/core/src/analysis/memory.ts` `suppressionStands`, enforced in `pipeline.ts`): a covered suppression stands on a file triaged critical only for a **learned** dismissal whose `learned_level` is `critical` (migration 0021: the triage level of the dismissed finding's file in the stored document, read by the learner from the document, never offered to the model, never from a request; entries learned before rank as non-critical) and only within the same file or directory as its evidence; never on a rule-floored file; the severity cap holds (a critical finding is never hidden); member-written entries are not eligible (and one claiming a level is dropped by `usableMemory`); everything else on a critical file is restored with the double-check marker as before. Visibility (schema 1.6, `document.hidden`, `HiddenFinding`/`HiddenByDismissal`, pipeline-written from the checked suppressions and the stored entry, held to a draft's location rules and `MEMORY_REF_MISMATCH`, covered by the final leak check, kept on unit reuse, raised as a draft when its entry cannot be cited): every standing suppression on any file is listed. Web: "N findings hidden by memory · show" under the file's AI strip (`FileView`, `.hidden-memory`), expanding to `HiddenCard`s (AI finding, severity, confidence, the dismissal that hid it, **Restore as a draft**); the panel's collapsed "Hidden by memory" section, items jumping to the file on the list; the restored draft says "Rocky had hidden it; you restored it". Restore: `DraftAction` `restore` on the drafts route creates the hidden finding's decision row (undecided); the row's existence is "restored" (`listReviewDrafts` lists it, `decideDraft` refuses any other action without it: `unknown_finding`; a plain draft cannot be restored: `invalid_transition`); `restoredAsDraft`/`draftableFindings` give the page, the posting plan and the learner the document's text and location, so a restored finding accepted and posted teaches a convention that supersedes the dismissal. ### P10-4 follow-ups

- Ask about the pull request and the repository index are off in token mode (they read code through the App installation); give them the token opener.
- A new sign-in link needs a server restart; a CLI (`pnpm rocky sign-in-link` or a compose `exec`) would avoid the restart.
- `packages/mirror` has no tests of its own; `syncOpenPullRequests` is covered through the server's token-mode suite (listing, merge detection, a vanished pull request closed). Move the cases into the package (left for later in the review fix round: a test move, no behaviour).
- Review fix round, left as a follow-up: a local process on the server can lock the loopback client address out of the sign-in link (20 wrong tries, `127.0.0.1` is everyone on a loopback install). A restart prints a new link and clears the lockout; a per-link lockout bound or a CLI to print a fresh link would avoid the restart.
- `deploy/init-env.sh` only accepts https addresses; accept `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` with `--token-mode` (and set `ROCKY_TRUST_PROXY=none`).
- A fine-grained token's pull-request write permission is only proven at the first post (no read-only probe); surface GitHub's refusal there with the token hint.
- The sync lock and throttles are in-process: fine for the single compose server, not for several replicas.
- Web: after 10 re-fetches while the server still says `running`, the inbox sync bar keeps showing "Syncing…"; show "Still syncing, reload later". The `/connection` link lives in the inbox sync bar only; drive a nav entry from `session.connection`.
- UI verification nits: the "Private" pill on `/connection` blends into the row (`tone-low` on `--panel-2`); the wizard's token step links `/connection` twice (step 2 "Add repositories", step 4); the token field's focus ring touches its label (`.token-field` gap); "Stop following" uses a red fill for a reversible action. Coverage: the axe sweep does not include the wizard token steps, the confirmations or token-rejected states, and no baseline shows token-rejected.
- Two Playwright tests fail on `main` as well (seen while verifying P10-4, unrelated to it): `shell.e2e.spec.ts:377` (Review panel drawer focus trap at 1024 px: focus lands on "1 finding hidden by memory · show") and `drafts.e2e.spec.ts:260` (a refused comment reopens the composer: click times out).

### P10-2 follow-ups

- Re-review nits: `SetupConfig.credentialsWritten` (boot-time boolean) is now only read by its
  test, since `server.ts` checks the file per request; drop it or make it the function. The
  announcement does not repeat when the same text comes twice within 5 s (clear and re-set on
  the next tick). No e2e for a double click on Finish while it is finishing (same guard as the
  test call; a repeated close is harmless). `clientKey` runs twice on token-redeem requests, so a
  missing proxy header counts twice in the fallback warning's number.
- **Needs human: the timed fresh-VM run** (`docs/self-host/setup.md`, "Checklist for a timed
  run"): a VM with a DNS name or a local VM with the quick tunnel, the GitHub click to create and install the app, one live LLM test
  call and one analysed pull request. Record the time and the 14 screenshots in the PR.
- The wizard's test call is not recorded in `llm_usage` (no org exists to count it against). If
  self-host reporting ever needs instance-level spend, add a non-tenant usage row for it.
- `GET /app/installations` reads one page of 20; an instance installed on more accounts shows the
  first 20 in the wizard only.
- Instance credentials stay in a 0600 file appended to `deploy/rocky.env` (ADR 0044 §5); storing
  them envelope-encrypted (ADR 0008's open question) is still open.
- Per-org LLM overlays and BYOK (ADR 0012 §7) are not part of self-host setup; they stay with C-5.
- The worker restarts in a loop during setup mode (no app id yet). Harmless, but noisy in
  `docker compose ps`; a worker "wait for the app" mode would be quieter.
- The setup token reaches Docker's log files through stderr (ADR 0044, residual risks). A
  `ROCKY_SETUP_TOKEN_FILE` (write the link to a file in `deploy/setup` instead) would keep it out.
- Fix round, not done:
  - Under `pnpm dev` the server cannot know Vite's origin, so `/setup` on the server answers a
    404 page that says to set `ROCKY_PUBLIC_URL=http://127.0.0.1:5173` instead of redirecting
    (`.env.example` says the same).
  - The quick-tunnel variant (`setup.md`) relies on Cloudflare's documented `X-Forwarded-For`
    behaviour; it has not been observed here. The guide has a check (`proxy_header_fallback` in the
    server's log) for the human run.
  - The images have not been built on arm64 (a VM on Apple silicon); the human run is the first.
  - The finished page's `docker compose …` command can wrap at a hyphen on narrow screens
    (cosmetic).

### P10-3 follow-ups

- **Integration with P10-1.** Done by P10-2: `docs/self-host/README.md` links `deploy/README.md`
  and `setup.md`, `deploy/README.md` links back; the bare-metal commands stay as they are.
- **Needs human: measure local models.** `docs/self-host/local-models.md` has no measured numbers
  on purpose. Run `pnpm evals run --injection --memory --ask` against one or two local setups
  (for example an Ollama model per role, and an OpenAI-compatible embedding model), publish the
  reports next to a hosted baseline, and add the results and the hardware they ran on to the
  page.
- **No schema-version check at boot.** The server and worker start on a database whose ledger is
  behind (or ahead of) their checkout and fail at the first query that needs the change. A boot
  check against `app.schema_migrations` (refuse when the ledger does not match the code's
  migrations) would make the upgrade order fail closed.
- **`pg_dump` does not carry `REVOKE USAGE ON LANGUAGE sql`** (seen on Postgres 17 in the restore
  drill; plpgsql's and the catalog functions' ACLs did come back). The docs say to re-run the
  provisioning before a restore and to check `pg_language`. The P2 follow-up "repeat the
  provisioning checks on every `migrate()` run or at server start" would catch a restore that
  skipped it.
- **No KEK re-wrap command.** `rewrapDataKeys` (`packages/crypto/src/keyring.ts`) exists only as a
  library function, so rotation today is "add a key and make it current", and no KEK can ever be
  removed. Add a `pnpm` command that re-wraps every org's data key under the current KEK (per org,
  compare-and-swap through `replaceOrgDataKey`) and reports which KEK ids are still in use.
- **KEK loss needs a manual recovery** (`docs/self-host/backups.md`, "The encryption keys"):
  sign-in opens the org's existing wrapped data key, so after losing the KEKs every org with a
  data key fails with `unknown_key_version` until the operator clears the encrypted columns by
  hand. The SQL there follows the code paths (member removal, first data key) but has not been
  drilled. A supported command (or sign-in treating an unknown KEK as "re-provision after
  confirmation") and a drill would close it.
- **Empty is not unset everywhere.** `PORT` (`""` → port 0, random), `HOST`, `LOG_LEVEL` and
  `DATABASE_URL` use `??`, so an empty value is used as given (`apps/server/src/server.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/config.ts`, `packages/log/src/logger.ts`); the reference lists them as
  exceptions. Make them treat empty as unset like every other variable, and make the server
  refuse an unset `DATABASE_URL` instead of falling back to the driver's `PG*` variables.
- **No `start` script or compiled build** for the server and worker; production runs them through
  `tsx`, as development does.
- ADR 0040 §1's table names the self-hosted embedding settings `ROCKY_LLM_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_*`;
  the variables are `ROCKY_LLM_OPENAI_*` (`packages/llm/src/env.ts`, the reference). Fix the ADR
  text.
- `GITHUB_APP_SLUG` is written to the credentials file and named in the setup page, but nothing
  reads it. Either read it (for an install link) or stop writing it.
- The link check does not fetch external URLs (no network in `pnpm verify`). A scheduled job
  could check them.

### deps-anthropic-sdk-1x follow-ups

- **Needs human: the pre-4.7 models on Bedrock and Vertex.** Bedrock takes API version
  `2026-10-21` for Claude 4.7 and later only, and Vertex's rule is not published, so the adapters
  keep the catalogue's pre-4.7 models on each platform's previous wire version
  (`anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` for `claude-haiku-4-5` on Bedrock; body
  `anthropic_version: vertex-2023-10-16` for Opus 4.6/4.5, Sonnet 4.6 and Haiku 4.5 on Vertex;
  ADR 0013 amendment "1.x SDKs" §3–4). The alternatives are to drop those models from the
  platform catalogues (`packages/llm/src/providers.ts`) and delete `PRE_4_7_MODELS` /
  `previousVersionFor` in `claude-platforms.ts`, or, once Vertex is confirmed to serve
  `2026-10-21` for them, to drop only the Vertex override. Neither was verified live (no accounts).
- Fix round (reviewer): should-fix 1 (Vertex pre-4.7 pin) and 2 (bearer-mode Bedrock case, U3
  wording) applied; nits (a) release ages, (b) the chain predates 1.x, (c) a `platformModel` case
  per platform, (d) `ANTHROPIC_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE` and `ANTHROPIC_SCOPE` planted, all applied.

  `ask` role; `--max-tokens 600000`). Its `ask injection:` line must read `leaks 0, citations
outside 0, markup 0`; `echoed` is how often the real model obeyed a payload (R1). Not run here
  (no live calls).

  `src/ask/`, `src/run/`, `baselines/fake.json`, `evals.yml`, `CODEOWNERS`).

  per member, `ask.org` 60/min and `ask.org.day` 600/day per org; raise
  `ROCKY_RATE_LIMIT_ASK_ORG_DAY` (and `_ASK_DAY`) for a large org.
- `llm_usage` does not refuse a row pairing a repository with another repository's pull request
  (fix round, N2; ADR 0042 R5): a three-column key dangles `pull_request_id` when the repository
  is deleted. A trigger checking the pair on INSERT, or a deferred check, would close it; today
  the two callers pass one `loadAskTarget` row's ids.
- A Postgres-backed `RateLimitStore` (ADR 0025 R1) matters more now: the day budget resets with
  the process and multiplies by the instance count.
- A per-token org budget (charged after each call from `MeteredTotals`) could sit beside the
  question counts if questions turn out to vary a lot in cost.
- The `poison` repair round's HTML and shell command are served as text (inert in the panel's
  text-only renderer); a content rule for shell pipelines in answers (as R2 suggests for agent
  prompts) is a product decision.
- Analysis rows could carry `repo_id` and `pull_request_id` too (one `meterRoles` option in
  `apps/worker/src/analysis.ts`), so P11 can bill per repository from one column.
- `Reply` is exported from `AskPanel.tsx` only for its unit test; a `problem-text.ts` module would
  be cleaner.

### P9-1 follow-ups

- **Needs human: live timing.** Run [the runbook](runbooks/index-live-timing.md) with a real
  key and a ~100k-line repository and record the two durations here. The offline numbers above
  hold the targets with a wide margin, but the provider's real latency and rate limits are not
  measured.
- **Needs human: prices.** OpenAI and Voyage AI embedding calls are metered with
  `cost_status = no_price` until a maintainer adds a verified price table version
  (`packages/llm/src/prices.ts`) from the providers' published pages.
- **Needs human: the `push` subscription.** The app manifest now subscribes to `push` (no new
  permission). Existing installations, including the dogfooding one, must accept the new event
  on GitHub before pushes reach Rocky; until then nothing enqueues an index.
- **Initial index without a push.** Today only a push to the default branch queues an index. A
  first index on installation (or when a repository is enabled), and an "Index now" control, are
  not built; the over-limit approval (`approveCodeIndex`) has no UI or CLI yet either.
- **HNSW write cost.** Maintaining the HNSW index is most of a full index's database time
  (`pnpm index:bench --without-hnsw`). If writes become the bottleneck, build approximate search
  only for repositories past the exact-search size.
- ~~Reuse by text hash~~: fixed in the review fix round (reuse is keyed by the sha256 of the
  embedded input, path included; a rename re-embeds).
- Review nits (fix round):
  - Voyage has a per-request token cap: batch by estimated tokens as well as by count (check the
    limit in Voyage's docs).
  - Archive mode ignores `.gitattributes` `export-ignore`/`export-subst`: fall back to blob reads
    for planned paths the archive did not yield.
  - A `not_found` blob or a missing archive entry leaves the stored row stale until the next run.
  - `finishCodeIndex` `over_limit` ignores `generation`, so a push that arrived meanwhile is not
    requeued.
  - ~~`writeIndexedFiles` accepts all-zero vectors~~: refused now, as for a query.
  - Search tests: add a second repository of the same org, and prove the HNSW iterative-scan
    path ran in approximate mode. pgvector ≥ 0.8 is required (now in ADR 0040 §3).
  - The planted-marker dump covers 4 tables: widen it to every table (`embeddings.source_ref`,
    `audit_events`, `webhook_deliveries` included).
  - `MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES` (8 GiB) is loose: derive the cap from the repository size, which the
    truncated-run gate now reads.
  - ~~`reconcilePush` session close without a catch~~: `.catch(() => undefined)` added.
  - ~~Grammar WASM integrity~~: one line in ADR 0040 §4 (exact pins, lockfile sha512).
- **Memory embeddings** (P8) are still not written; when they are, they use the same column,
  width and space rule (ADR 0040 §2–§3).
- **`OrgErasers.embeddings`** stays `nothingStoredYet`: vectors live in Postgres and go with the
  org's cascade; the slot is for an external vector store, which there is none of.
- ~~Purge on disable~~: decided in P9-2 (ADR 0041 §6): a disabled repository's index is kept,
  never served or updated, and erased with the org.
- OpenAI answers an exhausted credit balance with 429 `insufficient_quota`, which the adapters map
  to `rate_limited` (retryable): an index or analysis run then retries until its attempts run out
  instead of failing at once with a clear code. Map `insufficient_quota` to a non-retryable kind.

### P8-4 follow-ups

- **Migration numbering:** this task was assigned 0020 with 0019 reserved by a parallel task, but
  the migrator refuses gaps (`versions must be 1..n`), so it was renumbered to 0019 because it was
  ready first. A later memory task takes the next free number when it is rebased.
- ~~**P8-3's disabled switch** (ADR 0038 §4)~~: done in P8-3 (switched-off entries and
  repositories are exempt from decay and retirement).
- `decayed_at` is not exposed through `MemoryEntry`; P8-3 can show "fading since …" from it.
- Windows are per deployment (`app.retention_settings`), not per org or repository; a
  per-repository window is a P8-3 setting if usage shows the need.
- Member-written history (every P8-3 edit through supersede) is never purged by the job and
  accumulates until P8-3 deletes it, bounded by the per-repository cap.
- The audit row holds counts, not the ids of the retired entries (a 10 000-row batch would not
  fit the 4 KiB metadata cap); a per-entry `memory.retired` event is possible if P8-3 wants a
  history of what went.
- ADR 0036 nit 6 (a crafted `superseded_by` loop) stays open: the decay job neither hangs on
  nor deletes one (test), but a cycle-proof trigger is still not written.
- A re-scrub of older entries with the current member list (ADR 0036 residual risk, nit 10)
  is not part of this job; it would be a fourth rule here or a P8-3 action.
- Review nit N5: retiring a higher-role learned entry lifts ADR 0037's "overruled" protection
  (`memory-learning.ts`, `overruled`): a member's opposite dismissal about that pattern can be
  learned once the owner's entry is gone after a year without a citation (ADR 0038 §2 says so).
  If that should hold longer, exempt entries with `learner_role` owner/admin from retirement.
- Review nit N6: a dismissal that keeps being applied bumps `last_used_at` every time and never
  decays or retires; superseding it (P8-2's newer decision, P8-3's edit or delete) is the only
  bound. A cap on the age of an entry regardless of use, or a P8-3 "review this entry" nudge
  after N applications, if usage shows dismissals outliving their reason.
- Fix round 2 (fixed): the job never waits on a `memory_entries` row (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`
  in every rule, the chain behind a retiring head locked the same way and skipped whole when a
  row is held elsewhere); `touchMemoryEntries` sorts its ids; the race tests hold the other
  transaction open and assert the skip, no timing. **Remaining 40P01 paths:** none on
  `memory_entries` from the job's side. The job can still wait on an `embeddings` row (the
  `ON DELETE CASCADE` runs as the owner; nothing writes embeddings until P9-1) and holds a
  `KEY SHARE` on the org row for its audit insert; a deadlock there needs the other transaction
  to also wait on a row the job holds. Between other writers, `id = any()` statements acquire
  row locks in scan order, not array order, so sorting the touch's ids is a mitigation, not a
  guarantee, against the learner's supersedes (which lock in id order, two rows at a time).
- Fix round 1 (fixed): S1 floors enforced in `checkWindows` for every caller (`MEMORY_DECAY_MIN_DAYS`);
  S2 lock-first in every rule (two-connection race tests); N1/N2/N3/N7 doc and audit
  corrections (four steps before retirement; the audit row records the clamped instant; the
  batch bounds candidates, not chain rows; the docstring lists the rules in run order).

### P8-3 follow-ups

- No link to the memory page yet: it is reached at `/:owner/:repo/memory`. Add "Manage project
  memory" to the briefing's "Project memory applied" section (and the inbox per repository); this
  changes the briefing screenshot baselines, so it was left out of P8-3.
- Writing a new entry by hand (not an edit) needs evidence (path, commit, lines, PR): best from a
  PR page, where it is at hand.
- Import of `.rocky/context.md` (`parseMemoryContext` already reads it back) is not built.
- A switched-off entry stops learning only for the same kind, path and subject; a reworded title
  or a sibling path is learned anew (ADR 0039 residual risks).
- Re-scrub of untouched entries with the current member list is still open (ADR 0036 residual).
- The list shows the newest 500 entries (`truncated`); paging is not built. The export has all.
- Finding-level `memory` refs on draft cards could link to the entry on the memory page.
- Review nits (fix round 1): editing a switched-off entry sets the new row's `disabled_at`
  without touching `updated_at`; the cross-repository route test asserts 404 but not that the
  entry is unchanged; the store's role re-check (a role lowered between the route check and the
  transaction) is tested in the store, not through the routes.
- (from P8-2) Beside an edited entry that is not switched off, learning still adds an enabled
  opposite decision or a near-duplicate, because learning never supersedes member-written rows.
- [x] Decay versus switched-off entries (done after the rebase onto P8-4): the decay and retire
      scans in `packages/db/src/memory-decay.ts` skip `disabled_at is not null` and repositories
      with memory off, and both measure idleness from the last switch-on (`enabled_at`, fix
      round 4), so the time off never counts (ADR 0038 §4, ADR 0039).
- The export property test's random alphabet essentially never produces `http://` or `www.`;
  mixing tokens such as `"http://"`, `"www."`, `"@"` and `"a.b"` into the arbitrary would make
  it meaningful for autolinks (the explicit tricky cases cover them today).
- Switching a learned-switched-off entry on again is per entry: an opposite decision learned
  switched off (ADR 0039) must be switched on by hand.

- **Label:** the PR changes protected eval files (`baselines/fake.json`, `src/memory/run.ts`,

  is P8-4's `memory_decay`, 0020 P8-3's). The migrator refuses gaps (`loadMigrations`), so until
  both land `pnpm verify` needs untracked no-op placeholders for 0019/0020 (used locally, not
  committed); rename to the next free number if the order changes.
- **Live evidence (needs-human):** ADR 0037 §6 says a change to project memory needs a live
  memory run again (`pnpm evals run --memory` with a provider key). The new rule is exercised on
  the fake only; a live trial where run 2 is triaged `critical` would show whether a real model
  keeps the pattern in `suppressed` on a critical file (unpinned, with the entry learned on a
  critical run 1).
- Restore is one-way (no "hide again"): dismissing a restored finding teaches the dismissal
  again, which is the same decision. A "hide again" that deletes the row could be added to the
  dismissed state if dogfooding asks for it.
- The panel's "Hidden by memory" section lists hidden findings over the whole PR; a count per
  file in the sidebar (next to the severity dots) was not added.
- The CLI (`packages/evals/src/run/cli.ts` report lines) prints hidden counts; `docs/cli.md`'s
  document renderer does not list `hidden` findings yet.
- `hiddenOnCritical` and `memoryHiddenOnCritical` count suppressions, `hidden` counts the
  document's list: they differ when a hidden finding's entry could not be cited (raised as a
  draft instead) or when a unit was reused.
- A member-written dismissal (P8-3) never stands on a critical file; if P8-3 wants a person's
  rule to apply there, it needs its own explicit flag, reviewed by an admin, not `learned_level`.
- Fix round 1 (security review clean; reviewer: no blockers) applied: S1 the ADR's residual
  risks now say what holds on critical files, and the M3a widening is blocked in code (a
  higher role's entry of the opposite kind in the file's directory restores the finding with the
  double-check marker; directory + opposite kind, no subject match, justified in ADR 0037 §7);
  S2 root-level evidence stands on its own file only, `src/a` is not `src/ab`, a renamed file is
  judged by its new path; the hidden list is capped at `MAX_FINDINGS` with the overflow raised as
  drafts; a standing suppression that repeats a raised finding is not listed twice;
  `applyKnownFields` drops a model-supplied `hidden`/`restored`.
- UI verifier (fix round 1; no blockers: keyboard path and axe clean at every size, 168/168
  visual tests): applied: the restored card is lifted clear of the toast (`liftAboveToast` in
  `DiffBody`, for every card jump); the hidden card's focus ring is `--you`; baselines for the
  restored card (`drafts-restored-*`) and the panel's expanded list
  (`drafts-panel-hidden-open-*`). Nits not fixed: the collapsed line has a wide gap before
  "· show/hide" (the sr-only " on this file" span); the panel's "Hidden by memory" heading is
  followed by a toggle that repeats "… hidden by memory"; the panel section sits between
  Decided and the dismissal-learning note that belongs to Decided; in `brief-map-unavailable-*`
  the "Read by hand … drafted nothing" line does not mention the file's hidden finding.
- Flake follow-up (not caused here; reproduced on main under heavy local load): four
  `drafts.e2e.spec.ts` tests fail when the machine is loaded ("edit → save → refresh",
  "dismiss with a reason → refresh", "own comments: add → refresh", "a comment the server
  refuses"). Two read `reviewApi.decision("F1")` synchronously after the UI updates (a test
  race; `expect.poll` would fix them); two time out on "Comment on line 10" as detached or not
  stable (the virtualised row re-measures under load).
- Fix round 2 applied: a reused unit re-checks `suppressionStands` for its hidden findings
  (the unit key covers the file's selection, not the entries `overruledNearby` reads), restoring
  with the double-check marker; owner/admin-taught conventions and decisions load under their
  own bound (`learnedOverruling: 50`, `MAX_ANALYSIS_MEMORY` 300) so a member's accepts cannot
  push an owner's older convention out of the window (chosen over failing closed on a truncated
  learned-other group, which is routine in a busy repository).
- Rebased onto P8-4 (0019) and P8-3 (0020): one conflict (the learner's post-create update,
  merged into one statement with `learned_subject`). Interactions decided (ADR 0037 §7): an
  edited entry keeps the decision's role and blocks the critical path like the learned one; it
  never carries `learned_level` (the 0021 CHECK), so it never stands; switched-off entries and
  repositories with memory off are not loaded, so they neither stand nor block; the decay job
  and `learned_subject` are untouched.
- Fix round 3 applied: on reuse every applied entry is re-checked with `suppressionStands`
  (not only those with a hidden finding); the ADR says the marker a repeated draft would carry
  cannot be rebuilt on reuse, and that the owner/admin load group is shared.
- Nit (stats only, predates P8-2b): on reuse, suppressions that still stand are not counted in
  `memorySuppressed` or `memoryHiddenOnCritical` (a reused unit counts nothing it reuses).
- Nit: a hidden finding overflowing to a draft (an uncitable entry, or past the hidden cap) can
  take the draft list past `MAX_FINDINGS` (`raw` is not capped; not a regression: restored
  drafts could already do it).
- Nit: learned entries from before migration 0018 carry no role and rank as member, so an
  owner's convention learned before 0018 never blocks the critical path; more than 50 current
  owner/admin conventions and decisions in one repository drop the oldest out of the load group.
- Nit: ids are not checked across `findings` and `hidden` (`integrity.ts`); the pipeline keeps
  the collections apart (`F…` and `H…`), and a document with an `F1` in both would parse.
- Nit: the reuse filter (`pipeline.ts`, `reused.hidden.filter(offered)`) drops a hidden finding
  whose entry is no longer offered instead of raising it as a draft; in practice the unit key
  changes with the selection, so the unit is not reused then.
- Review nits go here.
- Post-rebase verification nit: an edited owner/admin convention has `origin = 'member'`, so it
  loads under the member bound (100), not `learnedOverruling`; an admin writing 100 newer entries
  pushes it out of the window and it stops blocking. Only admins write such rows. A partition for
  `origin = 'member' and learner_role in ('owner','admin') and kind <> 'dismissal'` (or folding it
  into the overruling group) would close it.
- Post-rebase verification nit: no test for a changed-kind edit (a learned member dismissal edited
  into a convention by an admin carries `admin` and blocks a member's critical dismissal nearby),
  and no core test that an edited dismissal carrying a role does not block (guaranteed today by
  `other.kind !== "dismissal"`).
