  One case is not a rate: more patterns (another directory, a near-miss that must still be
  raised) would measure over- and under-application.
- P8-3: show learned entries with their origin and the decision they came from, and let an admin
  delete or edit them (suppression is the feature, ADR 0037 residual 1); finding-level `memory`
  refs are in the document but only the briefing's "memory applied" list is shown today.
- Learning is best effort (separate transaction after the review is recorded): a crash in between
  loses that review's lessons. A queued "learn" job keyed by submission id would make it durable.
- The mirror stores no PR commenters or requested reviewers, so `people` is the PR author and the
  reviewer (plus every member login the store adds). If the mirror grows commenters, pass them.
- Fix round applied: joint-quote final check (refs out of model text, offered entry texts
  allowed); suppression enforced by the pipeline with a per-entry severity cap (migration 0018);
  no dismissal learned from the reviewer's own PR; learner role recorded and respected by
  supersede; member/learned-dismissal/learned-other load bounds; relevance ≥ 1 for every kind,
  stable handles; triage reused when only memory changed (`FileUnit.triageKey`); repository lock
  for learning; dismissals learned before accepts; corroboration only from another PR.
- N3: root-level files count as one directory for relevance (`dirname` is `""` for all of
  them), so an entry on `README.md` is offered to every other root-level file at relevance 2.
- N4: `REVIEW_MEMORY_INSTRUCTIONS` joined `PROMPT_TEXTS`, which widens the leak guard for every
  stage, without a `PIPELINE_VERSION` bump: reused units are re-checked against the wider guard
  at reuse (a unit that now trips it is analysed again), so no stored unit is wrong, but a bump
  would make the change explicit. **Done in P8-2b** (`p8-2b.1`).
- N5: learning runs on the post request path (after the review is recorded, in its own
  transaction): it adds latency to "Finish review". A queued job would take it off the path.
- N7: a failed learning attempt is never retried (logged as `memory_learn_failed`); the queued
  job above would also give it retries.
- The severity cap compares the severity the model gives a suppressed finding; a model that
  downgrades a finding to fit under a dismissal is not caught by it (ADR 0037 residuals).
- A suppression restored as a draft can bring a file above 25 findings (the per-answer cap
  counts `findings` and `suppressed` separately); the document's 200-finding cap still holds.
- "Dismissals before accepts within the cap" has no test with more than 25 decisions (the
  pr-847 fixture has ten findings).
- The estimate counts a file whose triage would be reused as a whole analysis (upper bound).
- Fix round 2 applied: a lower role's opposite decision about a higher role's learned pattern is
  not stored; no suppression stands on a file triaged critical or with a rule floor (triage never
  sees memory); a restored item that repeats a raised finding is stored once. `readByHand` was
  not extended: with no suppression standing on risky files, a risky file can only look clean
  by having no findings at all, which `readByHand` already lists.
- N2 (round 2): restored and raised drafts together can exceed 25 per file; only the document's
  200-finding cap bounds them.
- N3 (round 2): the final-check allowance is every offered entry's text, wider than needed
  (each finding could be allowed only its own file's selection).
- N5 (round 2): the estimate ignores reused triage (counts the triage call anyway).
- N6 (round 2): suppressed items cost output tokens under the unchanged review output budget
  (`MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS.review`); a file with many suppressions may hit the cap and be truncated.
- `supersedeMemoryEntry` gained `origin: "learned"` in P8-1's file (ADR 0037 §3); P8-1's own
  tests do not cover it (P8-2's `memory-learning.test.ts` does).
- `containsPromptCanary` and `containsPromptCanaryFragment` now also cover the memory canary
  (P8-1's helpers, extended here; `prepareMemoryEntry` refuses eight characters of either).
- Review nits go here.

### H4-16 follow-ups

- Review nit: a preload refused with `rate_limited` is retried when the first file opens, which
  usually lands in the same one-minute window and is refused again (one more read of the
  member's limit). Waiting for `Retry-After` before the retry, or not retrying 429, would avoid it.
- Review nit (product tradeoff, to confirm with the maintainer): the org's `diff.org` budget
  (15 GitHub reads a minute) is now also spent by people who only skim a briefing and never open
  a file. In a busy org that could leave less budget for people reading diffs.
- Done in the fix round: the e2e asserts exactly one preload request.
- `DiffProvider.test.tsx` renders to static markup like the other web component tests, so effects
  do not run: that the provider aborts its load on unmount or on a new source or PR (the
  `useEffect` cleanup that calls `loader.dispose()`) is covered only through `dispose` in
  `diff-loader.test.ts`. A DOM test environment for `apps/web` would let it be tested directly.

### P8-1 follow-ups

- Fix round (security M1, M2, L1; review S1–S5) applied: the canary refusal uses the leak
  guard's folding (`containsPromptCanary`) on text and evidence path; known people are ordered
  PR author → member logins → caller's list, deduped case-folded, refused past 5 000 distinct
  (`too_many_people`) instead of truncated; short or common-word logins match only in person
  positions (`matchesAsBareWord`); CamelCase words, plural-noun verbs and inline code are not
  name contexts; dotted and CamelCase decorators are not handles; `origin` is a caller parameter
  and `memory.edit` is re-checked `FOR SHARE` for member-written entries and supersedes; a
  partial index on `(org_id, superseded_by)`.
- Fix round 2 (verification review) applied: weakness is the dictionary or ≤ 2 letters only
  (`john`, `bob`, `Eric Li`, `Pérez` are scrubbed bare; the property test covers 3–4-letter
  logins); cue and honorific names keep the permissive shape with a word-end guard (no
  `[person]Shawn`), the speech-verb shape allows hyphenated parts (`Jean-Luc said`); unlisted
  `@CamelCase` handles are people unless alone on a line or listed; listed people and `@handles`
  are scrubbed inside backticks (only the context heuristics skip code); `MAX_KNOWN_PEOPLE` is
  50 000 distinct folded names with a timing test; any eight-character run of the canary is
  refused (`containsPromptCanaryFragment`).
- Fix round 3 applied: known people are matched by word lookup (an index of names and display
  name parts, no compiled alternation: linear in the list, measured at the cap with five- and
  twelve-word names); display-name parts are case-insensitive; weak names in person positions
  are scrubbed inside backticks; cue/honorific and speech-verb shapes accept `O'Brien`,
  `De'Andre`, `McDonald`, `DeShawn`; the ADR's guarantee, the `Mark the field` example and the
  50 000 note were corrected, and the own-line CamelCase handle and brand-after-cue false
  positives listed.
- Fix round 4 applied: every display-name part is indexed, trimmed of punctuation, with
  hyphenated sub-parts; the index is keyed by the first two words (the shared-first-word worst
  case); the ADR's guarantee and timings follow the verifier's measurements.
- Fix round 5 applied: the names, parts and sub-parts examined per call are capped at
  250 000 (`MAX_KNOWN_PEOPLE_ENTRIES`, `too_many_people` beyond: 50 000 three-word names fit,
  five-word or fifty-letter names do not), the index is keyed by the first three words and a key
  with more than 256 names is refused, so shared prefixes cannot go quadratic; the ADR carries
  the measured times and heap.
- Scrubber nits (not fixed): `alice'S` leaves `'S` (only a lower-case possessive is absorbed);
  the Cyrillic look-alike folding differs by case (`ИВАН` and `Иван` fold differently, so a
  listed `Ivan` matches one but not the other); `ilker`/`İlker` and `STRAUSS`/`Strauß` do not
  match (dotted İ and ß do not case-fold to the ASCII forms); CJK names without word boundaries
  (`李雷说` is one word to the tokeniser, so a listed `李雷` inside it is missed); heuristic
  false positives `MacBook said` / `VanDyke said` (the Mac/Van name prefixes).
- Security L2: chat-template tokens (`<|im_start|>`, `[INST]`, `<<SYS>>`, …) are not refused at
  storage; P8-2 must pass entries only as untrusted text (`LlmUntrustedText`), never
  concatenated into instructions.
- Security L3: `content` is updatable by the app role today (table-level UPDATE). Either a
  column-level UPDATE grant that excludes `content` (then `touch`/`supersede` keep working), or
  the rule "edit = insert + supersede" enforced by a trigger.
- Security L4: `@alice-` (trailing hyphen) and `@alice/` (a path-like mention) are not scrubbed
  as handles; `alice` itself is caught only when listed.
- Review nit 6: T41's "a cycle is impossible" rests on the repository's checks; a crafted UPDATE
  as the app role could still close a loop. A cycle-proof trigger (walk `superseded_by` under the
  row lock) or reword T41 to say so.
- Review nit 7: loopback and private IPs (`127.0.0.1`, `10.0.0.1`), four-part versions
  (`1.2.3.4`) and example domains (`alice@example.com` in docs) are scrubbed as if personal.
- Review nit 8: `cc: Bob` (a colon after the cue), `@carol(…)` (treated as a decorator) and
  hyphen variants of a login (`alice-dev` for `alice`) are not caught.
- Review nit 9: a partial index on `(org_id, superseded_by)` was added in 0017 (fix round).
- Review nit 10: scrubbing only at write time (ADR 0036 residual risks): a later member or a
  renamed login is not removed from older entries; a re-scrub job is a P8-3/P8-4 item.
- Review nit 11: never log `err.detail` from a memory store failure (Postgres echoes the row's
  values in `detail` for CHECK and unique violations); the server's P8-3 routes must reduce
  store errors to a SQLSTATE like `inboxDatabaseError` does.
- Review nit 12: a 0017-specific migration test (the CHECKs and the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL`
  are covered by `memory.test.ts` on the migrated schema; a test of the down script's exact
  0002 shape beyond the ACL snapshot in `migrate.test.ts` is not written).
- P8-2 must pass entries into prompts as `LlmUntrustedText` with their own canary (the leak
  guard's texts, see the P4-4 note on private prompt text), and pass the logins it saw around the
  evidence (commenters, reviewers) as `people` to `createMemoryEntry`.
- P8-3: delete and a per-repository "disabled" switch are not in the repository yet (the app
  role holds DELETE; `embeddings` cascade). `superseded_by … ON DELETE SET NULL` makes an older
  entry current again when the newer one is deleted: delete chains, or supersede instead.
- P8-4: `last_used_at`, `created_at`, `confidence` and `superseded_by` are the decay inputs; run
  per org inside `withOrg`. The per-repository cap (5 000) is a ceiling, not a budget.
- Scrubber limits (ADR 0036 §2): unlisted names in neutral sentences, scripts without letter case
  unless listed, "the CTO", IPv6, postal addresses, usernames in URLs (`github.com/alice`),
  `O'Brien`-style names after cues. A per-org "refuse any capitalised bigram" option is the next
  step if the exports show names. The `@`-tag allow-list is a list: unusual tags are scrubbed.
- `removeMember` keeps `members.login` (ADR 0024); "former member" is derived at read time, the
  login stays in `members` until the org is erased.
- The `at … dot …` email form takes the preceding word as the local part ("look at foo dot com"
  becomes `[email]`); harmless, noted.

### H4-17 follow-ups

- A selection across lines still copies a flag's visible text ("✓ Checked" and its note) between
  the code lines, and the text of draft cards and own comments it spans. If reviewers want only
  code there, make those notes unselectable while a selection crosses them (their own text would
  then need another way to be copied) or add a copy handler.
- An empty code line has no text to select, so a copied selection loses it (no blank line).
- The e2e suite runs Chromium only; the WebKit and Firefox copy checks were a one-off run. A
  small cross-browser project for `diff.e2e.spec.ts` "copied code is only code" would keep them.
- Review: Safari's real clipboard is unverified. Only `getSelection().toString()` was checked in
  WebKit (Playwright cannot read its clipboard); the maintainer checks it by hand. If Safari's
  copy still carries the sign words, the robust option is to draw the visible +/− as CSS
  `::before` content and keep the word in the sign column only for screen readers.
- Review: other `sr-only` text could be copied when a selection crosses it. The PostedPill's
  " to GitHub, read-only" (`DraftCard.tsx`) is now `no-copy` (fix round); the composer's
  `sr-only` label "Your comment on line N" (`DiffBody.tsx`) is not yet. `.no-copy` is only for
  invisible text (its CSS comment says so).
- Review nit: the e2e drag test picks lines by index (`code.slice(7, 12)` for lines 8–12 of
  `src/auth/invitations.ts`); a fixture change would break it. Selecting by `data-line` and
  reading those rows' code would not.

### H4-18 follow-ups

- At 16 px the tab icon is thin and light next to typical solid favicons (the inner hatching
  blurs to grey). A simpler, bolder small variant would read better; the website's unused
  `rocky-mountains-0x` candidates are a starting point.
- `favicon.svg` follows the OS/browser colour scheme, not Rocky's own light/dark toggle, so a
  light OS with a dark browser theme gives a dark-on-dark tab icon. A faint contrasting outline,
  or a tile behind the mark in the SVG, would keep it visible in every pairing.
- Review nit: the raw-colour allow-list exempts the whole of `apps/web/public/favicon.svg`, but
  `BrandMark.test.tsx` only collects `stroke: #hex;` declarations. Assert that every colour
  literal in the file (hex, colour functions, named colours) is one of the two `--text` values;
  the test also assumes light comes before dark in `tokens.css`.
- Review nit: "draws the same mountain" compares `d` attributes only; compare
  `(d, stroke-width)` pairs so the two marks cannot drift in weight.
- Review nit (hardening): the favicon's stroke comes only from its `<style>`. Put
  `stroke="#0f172a"` on the `<g>` and keep the `<style>` for the dark override, so the icon still
  draws if an engine ever blocks the style.
- Review note: `index.html` declares the ICO as `sizes="32x32"` although it holds 16/32/48, the
  usual way to make browsers prefer the SVG. Intended.

### H4-15 follow-ups

- `map-styles.e2e.spec.ts` "Flow playback › Play runs to the end" timed out once in a full e2e run with 4 workers (10.6 s; 7 s alone, passed 3/3 alone). A timing flake under load, unrelated to this change; its budget could take the run's CPU into account.
- Review nit: the sideways scrollers `.seq-scroll` (`change-map/map-styles.css`) and `.map-scroll` (`briefing/briefing.css`) are not positioned, so `sr-only` text inside them (e.g. `FlowPlayback.tsx`) belongs to `#main` and is not clipped by them. A flow wide enough to put a hidden label past `#main`'s right edge would give the main column a small sideways scroll (not reachable with today's data). Add `position: relative` to both.
- Review nit: the "page overflow" e2e group only opens a diff file; add the briefing with Flow playback at 375 px to cover the sideways map scrollers.
- Review nit: the comment above `.col` in `shell.css` says the drawers' `position: fixed` still wins; for the review panel the reason differs (`#review-panel` is `.panel`, not `.col`). Reword it.
- Worktrees that run DB-backed tests at the same time interfere through the one dev database (H4-14 and H4-15 did); a database or schema per worktree would stop it.

### H4-4 follow-ups

- **Needs human (CI):** the "evals (real models)" job has never run a model: `ROCKY_EVALS_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
  is not set in the `evals-live` or `evals-nightly` environment, so the job skips itself with a
  notice and reports success (every run so far, e.g. the nightly of 2026-10-31). Set the key
  (packages/evals/README.md, "CI") so provider-side schema rejections are caught.
- The live eval run exits 0 when a subject's analysis fails (`failed (internal_error)` is scored
  as 0 recall and printed), and the placeholder live baseline turns the gate into a warning. Make
  a failed subject fail the run (`packages/evals/src/run/cli.ts`, protected by the
  `evals-baseline-change` label), and commit the first real baseline.
- **Done (H4-5):** the graph is an enforced output again; it asks for lanes, nodes and edges only
  (ADR 0013 amendment H4-5). Flows, views and the walkthrough come back as their own calls with
  their UI (ADR 0021 amendment H4-5); each compiles on its own, the walkthrough alone pushed the
  grammar over its limit.
- The OpenAI-compatible adapter sends every output as a strict `json_schema` response format and
  does not honour `mode: "prompt"` (no role uses the mode since H4-5; the graph it sends has no
  recursive definition any more). Honour the mode there too if a role ever needs it.
- The SDK's transform folds `enum` and `const` into descriptions (`transform-json-schema.ts` pops
  neither), so enforced outputs are not grammar-constrained on enum values; the validator
  enforces them. A newer SDK may change this; the contract tests pin the sent schema shape.
- Review round 1 nits (recorded, not fixed): no leak-guard test covers the two new prompt texts
  (`SCHEMA_PREAMBLE`, `FINAL_INSTRUCTION_PROMPT_SCHEMA`); `systemPromptWithSchema` takes an
  unbranded string for the schema (only adapters call it, with `JSON.stringify` of the output's
  own `jsonSchema`); the `PIPELINE_VERSION` bump to p4-4.2 re-bills triage and review units
  although only the graph prompt changed (the version keys unit answers, not the graph);
  `PROMPT_HASH` excludes the graph schema, so a schema-only change does not show as a prompt
  change in eval reports (H4-5 changed `GRAPH_INSTRUCTIONS`, so its hash did change); `providerJsonSchema` does not recurse into `contains` or
  `unevaluatedItems`/`unevaluatedProperties` (Zod emits none); folding is not needed for a
  prompt-mode output (it is applied uniformly so a role can switch modes); the adapter contract
  tests exercise prompt mode with the fixtures' toy verdict schema, not the graph schema.
- The CLI's per-call reservation for the graph was 109,204 tokens on a tiny PR, so the default
  `--max-tokens` refuses small runs; the real run used 9,877 for the graph. Revisit the
  reservation (`apps/worker/src/cli/budget.ts`: schema bytes plus `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS.graph`) so
  the default cap fits a small PR. Budgets unchanged here and in H4-5 (the graph schema is now
  much smaller, but the 29-file dogfooding PR still needed `--max-tokens 3000000` to get past
  the review calls' reservations: four in flight at 64K output each plus their inputs; 400,000
  stopped the run at the first review call. The plan's "certain calls at their caps" was 780K).

### Dogfooding follow-ups (2026-11-01)

From the first review of a mid-size private dogfooding pull request: a money ledger with an
in-memory store behind a port, plus its tests.

- **Triage under-rates money and concurrency code.** Haiku rated the in-memory ledger store
  "normal" (42) as "test-only code". In fact it sits in `src/`, is exported, and is the reference
  behaviour a production adapter must copy. The tests that should prove its lock were also
  "normal". Neither reached the review stage. The independent reviewer's top finding lived
  there: the concurrency test passed with the lock removed. Rocky missed it. Add rules
  pre-triage floors (P4-3) like the existing auth-path rule:
  - paths matching `billing/`, `payments/`, `ledger`, `credit`, `invoice` or `money` are at
    least important;
  - so are files that implement a port or adapter for those;
  - tests of locking or concurrency are not auto-downgraded.

  Measure with an eval case built from this pull request's shape: a store whose lock the tests
  don't prove.

- **Done (H4-5): the change map shows logic only** (ADR 0014 amendment H4-5). Before: the graph
  call got every file that triage didn't mark as noise. That includes docs (ADRs, READMEs) and config (CI workflows,
  lint, tsconfig, package manifests), so they can appear as cards. Product rule: the map shows
  code with behaviour. Config or docs appear only when they change behaviour the code reads, for
  example a feature flag, a runtime setting, a migration or a schema. Do it in two places:
  - a deterministic filter on the graph's input (file category from the pre-triage rules);
  - a line in the graph instructions about config that feeds code.

  Pair this with the change-map scope decision (H4-4 follow-up): ask the model only for what the
  page draws today (lanes, nodes, edges), in enforced structured output. Flows, views and the
  walkthrough come back with their UI. (Both done in H4-5.)

- The walkthrough plays every stop over the whole map: a stop on a flow stage lights the flow's

- Flow playback scrolls sideways on phones; a vertical layout for narrow screens would read

- ui-verifier (fixed): walkthrough focus text unreadable at 375 (a focus is now never drawn

### H4-5 follow-ups

- Review round 1 (fixed): B1 doc stems matched code by stem (`security.ts` + `README.md` was
  "no-logic"); S1 tool stems matched any extension (`lib/jest.ts`); S2 `requirements` matched
  code; S3 `Contest.java` was a test; S4 directory rules applied anywhere in the path; S5 the
  scope was decided from triaged files only (an over-size or refused logic file fell back to
  the tests); S6 one stray file reference dropped a whole valid map; S7 every document was
  stamped 1.2.0. Now: anchored rules (ADR 0014 amendment H4-5, rule table), the scope over every
  non-noise file with `too-large`/`analysis-failed` instead of a fallback, `stripGraphFiles`
  with `stats.graphFilesStripped`, and `requiredSchemaVersion` (1.1.0 unless `no-logic`).
  Security low (fixed): the `no-logic` text speaks of the path rules, not of the code.
- Review round 1 nits (recorded, not fixed):
  - N1: `map-scope.test.ts` bounds the hostile-path scan by wall clock (now 2 s over five
    paths); a structural check (no unanchored regex over the whole path) would be stricter.
  - N2: `stripGraphFiles` runs before the integrity check, so its drops are not in the repair
    issue list and `MAX_REPAIR_ISSUES` applies to the integrity issues alone; the count is the
    only trace of a drop.
  - N3: `.envrc` (direnv, a developer tool) is kept as runtime config, and a non-doc file
    beside migrations or in a deploy tree is kept by the directory rule (`deploy/notes.md` is a
    doc; `deploy/plan.html` is kept).
  - N5: a node's `subtitle` or `summary` can still name an excluded file; only `files[]` is
    checked (ADR 0014 R10).
  - N6: there is no offline oracle for the provider's grammar-size limit; the real run is the
    evidence (ADR 0014 R8).
- **Flows as their own call (done in H4-7, with the walkthrough; ADR 0014 amendment H4-7; the UI is H4-6).** Flows come back as a separate
  Sonnet call with its own output schema (flows compile on their own, H4-4 probe), and a
  sequence-diagram view in the UI. It is the biggest remaining gain for review, at the cost of
  one more read of the mapped diffs per call (tens of thousands of input tokens on a mid-size
  PR): a sequence shows the order of checks (charge → BYOK skip → idempotency
  key → overdraft refusal), which a component map cannot. Same rules as the map: logic-only
  input, file and range refs stripped to the mapped set, the leak guard, validated by the
  document schema. Later options, with PR Lens (MIT) as the reference: drill-down views (a
  container view with component children, `defaultOpen` on the top one) and a walkthrough
  (three to seven change steps, each on one diagram with a focus); the walkthrough alone pushed
  the enforced grammar over its limit, so it is its own call too.
- Round 2 (done): `GRAPH_INSTRUCTIONS` grew from 925 to 3,135 characters (PR Lens's writer's
  guidance paraphrased, credited in the file header and `NOTICE`; a small example of Rocky's
  own); graph file references may carry a `range` in new-file line numbers, kept only inside
  the span of the file's new-side hunks (`stripGraphRefs`, `stats.graphRangesStripped`); a card
  with a range opens its file at the range's first line (`targets.ts`, ADR 0028 §5's `line`).
- The scope's table is deliberately narrow (ADR 0014 R7): a runtime file under a root
  `scripts/` or `tools/`, or a `robots.txt`, is left off the map. Amend the table from evidence,
  not guesses.
- The CLI's `--max-tokens` reservation (above, H4-4 follow-up) is the real blocker for mid-size
  PRs now that the graph is cheap: a 29-file PR needs a cap far above its real spend.
- Graph file references outside the PR are still allowed by the published schema (decision 6 in
  `docs/schema/analysis-document.md`); only Rocky's pipeline refuses them. If another producer
  ever writes documents, decide whether the schema should refuse them too.

### H4-8 follow-ups

- **Needs human (PR label):** `packages/evals/baselines/fake.json` (new prompt hash, review-role

  needs the `evals-baseline-change` label. Scores and injection totals are unchanged.
- **Needs human (dogfooding):** no live call was made. Re-run the analysis on a real PR and read
  the TL;DR: whether the model keeps to four short sentences and leaves out scaffolding is a
  prompt question the fakes cannot answer.
- The fake evals use 989K tokens; the default `--max-tokens` is 1M. Fix round: CI's harness step
  (`evals.yml`) and the README's regenerate command now pass `--max-tokens 2000000`, the cap
  the baseline was made with (as in H4-7). `evals.yml` is a protected file, so the label above
  covers it too.
- **N3:** a TL;DR bullet that names a finding id (F3) is not checked against the findings; an
  invented id would read as real. An integrity check (`UNKNOWN_FINDING_REF` on `F\d+` in the

- **N4:** the injection adversary's briefing leak payloads are smaller than before (two 160-char
  bullets and a 200-char reason instead of a 2 000-char summary and a 500-char reason). Each is
  still well over the leak guard's n-gram window, so the leak tests still exercise the guard; a
  later change that shrinks the fields further should re-check that.
- Fix round S1: TL;DR items are `line` text (no tab, U+2028 or U+2029 either), not prose with a
  newline rule.
- "One sentence" is asked for and bounded by length and the single-line rule, not counted; a model
  can still pack two short sentences into one bullet.
- `briefing.summary` is no longer written by the AI briefing; nothing else reads it except the
  CLI and the web, which fall back to it. A 2.0 schema could drop it once no stored document
  needs it.

### H4-7 follow-ups

- **Needs human (PR label):** `packages/evals/baselines/fake.json` was regenerated (the
  injection run now makes 279 graph-role calls; cap raised to 2M so the gate has headroom);
  the PR needs the `evals-baseline-change` label.
- **Needs human (dogfooding):** no live call was made here. Run `pnpm -s rocky analyze` on the
  private dogfooding PR to confirm the two enforced schemas compile on the provider (ADR 0014
  R12; a rejection is `llm_invalid_request`) and to read the real token counts
  (`stats.usageByStage.flows`/`walkthrough` in the worker log, the role table in the CLI).
- The walkthrough is flat (R13): no `stage` (a step cannot play over a flow), no lane focus, no
  message focus. When H4-6's styles want a step on a sequence, extend `WalkthroughStepAnswer`
  with an optional flow id and message ids, strip them against the stored flows, and re-check
  the grammar (the full `WalkthroughStep` compiled in the H4-7 probe with 6 optional fields).
- Payload samples (R15) and drill-down views are not asked for; views need the recursive `View`
  unrolled or a flat answer shape.
- The two calls run one after the other (the walkthrough names the flows). Running them in
  parallel would save a few seconds per analysis at the cost of a walkthrough blind to the
  flows and two reservations in flight under the CLI cap.
- `stats.graphFilesStripped`/`graphRangesStripped` now also count flow-step references; split
  them if a dashboard ever needs the map's cards apart from its flows.
- The web renders these in H4-6 (three map styles); the renderer (`packages/renderer`) still
  draws the architecture view only and ignores flows and the walkthrough.
- An unchanged `PIPELINE_VERSION`: the new calls are whole-PR, not units (as H4-5).
- Review round 1 (fixed): SF1 `stats.tokens` was redacted in the worker log (now
  `usageByStage` with `inputTokens`/`outputTokens`/`cacheRead`/`cacheWrite`, checked against
  the redactor in `analysis-stats-log.test.ts` and the e2e log); SF2 repair issue paths pointed
  into the stripped flows and steps (now rebased onto the model's answer, `rebaseFlowIssues`/
  `rebaseStepIssues`); security low / N3 the estimate did not count the graph-role re-reads
  (now the mapped diffs once per graph-role call, map stages only with a map); a regression
  test pins the final whole-document leak check over a copy spread across the graph, flows and
  walkthrough; N4 dollar figures replaced by token ranges; N2 the flows answer schema's maximum
  now matches the prompt's "one to three".
- Review round 1 nits (recorded, not fixed):
  - N1: a persistent retryable error (5xx) in an optional section (flows, walkthrough) is thrown
    like everywhere else, so the queue retries the whole run and the spend repeats (ADR 0014
    R4); a per-section fallback to `analysis-failed` after the provider's retries would keep
    the map. Decide with the checkpointing follow-up.
  - N2 (rest): the prompts' "two to eight participants" and "three to twelve nodes" are
    guidance, not the schema's limits (12 participants, 256 nodes); the answer schemas keep the
    document's bounds except the flow count.
  - N5: file and range drops on flow steps are counted in `graphFilesStripped`/
    `graphRangesStripped`; a separate `flowFilesStripped` would tell the cards from the steps.
  - N6: `stageOf` is exported from `@rocky-reviews/core` as well as `/testing`, because the
    evals' non-test scripts (`pr-847/script.ts`, `pr-912/fixture.ts`, `seeded/fake-model.ts`)
    answer by stage and the lint rule bans the testing entry outside tests; the pipeline
    itself does not call it. A `@rocky-reviews/core/evals` entry would let it leave the main
    index.
  - N7: in `completeWithRepair` the leak guard runs on the model's answer before the
    pipeline's check strips references, so a leaking label on an element that stripping would
    drop still costs the repair round; harmless (stripped text is never stored) but one call.

### H4-3 follow-ups

- **Needs human (dogfooding):** open a real PR's file on `rocky-reviews/rocky` with the server's
  `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` set and confirm GitHub issues the compare diff on a token minted with
  `contents: read` + `pull_requests: read` for one `repository_ids` entry (ADR 0010's residual
  risk: the compare permission is not verified against a live installation; a wrong assumption
  shows as 502 `forge_unavailable`, never as a diff).
- The diff served is not compared with the mirror's digest (`pr_revisions.diff_sha256`): a base
  branch rewritten between the analysis and the open, or a head without a recorded revision
  (GitHub's base used), can shift draft line numbers. Compare the digest for whole readings and
  show a note when it differs (ADR 0034 residual risks).
- The whole-files trim drops the last file of a `max_bytes` prefix even when the adapter's cut
  fell exactly on its end (it cannot tell without parsing). Parsing the prefix server-side would
  keep it.
- A cached diff outlives a `head_moved` by up to a minute: the page keeps the mirrored head's
  diff (what it says it shows) until the mirror catches up.
- The per-member `diff` limit and the diff cache are per process (ADR 0025 R1).
- The file view's "Sign in with GitHub" (a dead session) has no `returnTo`; the PR page's own
  sign-in state does. Pass the page's path into the diff context, or reload the page on 401.
- Token reuse per (installation, repository) for a few minutes (ADR 0011 A14) would now spare the
  mint for the diff as well as for the membership step; both share one forge instance.
- Review round 1 (fixed): SF1 the cache key held no base for a PR without a revision (two PRs
  from one head against different bases shared an entry; GitHub's live base was read); now the
  base is always the mirror's (`coalesce(v.base_sha, p.base_sha)`) and the key carries the PR
  number too. SF2 failures are remembered briefly (30 s / 10 s / 5 s), an org-wide `diff.org`
  limit (15 uncached reads a minute) sits next to the per-member one (now 10 a minute). SF3 the
  web tells `commits_unavailable` from `head_moved` by the 409's fixed code.
- Review round 1 nits (not done):
  - The cache's byte bound counts UTF-16 code units of the decoded text (`diff.length`), not the
    bytes read; a diff of mostly ASCII is counted about right, one of mostly non-ASCII under.
  - Eviction is first-in-first-out (insertion order), not least-recently-used: a hit does not
    move its entry to the end.
  - The truncated-diff e2e takes decisions on a combination production refuses: the analysis
    does not run on a truncated diff, so no drafts exist for one.
  - No test joins the real route to the web app (the db test checks the answer with the web's
    `parseDiffData`; the e2e answers from a mock); a boot-and-browse test is a follow-up.
  - `wholeFiles` on a prefix without any header at a line start (content before the first
    `diff --git`) returns the content before it; the adapter never produces such a prefix.
  - A `HEAD` request runs the full handler and mints a token (Fastify derives HEAD from GET).
- ui-verifier nits (not done): "Read it on GitHub" has no full stop and no ↗ hint; the file's
  AI summary still says "Accept, edit or dismiss each one inline" when no diff is shown; error
  lines (`role="alert"`) look the same as neutral notes; "Mark viewed" stays available when the
  diff could not be shown. Fixed: `.diff-status a` links use `--you` (were the browser default);
  baselines for the signed-out, rate-limited and commits-unavailable states.

### H4-1 follow-ups

- **Needs human (ops):** set `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (the same PEM the worker has) on the server's
  environment; production refuses to boot without it. In development, add it to `.env` (the
  credentials file from H3 already holds it) or the PR routes answer 503.
- **Needs human (product):** veto if the server should boot in production without the key (it
  would run with every repository route at 503 instead of refusing to start).
- `pullRequestApprover` is still not wired (P6-7/P3-5 follow-up, not approved): bulk approval
  answers 503 `approve_unavailable`.
- The access cache is per server process: a cross-instance broadcast (ADR 0011) before running
  more than one server; a worker-side removal does not need it (ADR 0033 §5).
- ADR 0011's live-installation confirmation of the membership step is still open; the first
  public or internal repository exercises it (denies, never allows, if GitHub answers otherwise).
- ADR 0011 A14 (installation rate limit): every public/internal question by a collaborator still
  mints and revokes a token from the server (non-collaborators are remembered 30 s, ADR 0033 W11);
  token reuse per (installation, repository) would remove the mint.
- Review nits (not done): `server.access.db.test.ts` covers the 200/404 wiring for the drafts
  `GET`, the review preview `GET` and the inbox; add the wired 200/404 cases for
  `POST …/approve-over-limit` (only its 503 is covered) and for the posting `POST` (the preview
  stands in for it). `githubAppAuthFromEnv` validates the key even when login is off (a malformed
  key refuses to boot although nothing would use it); acceptable, or skip the parse without
  login. The test file depends on test order (sessions, bob's cookie and GitHub state carry
  across tests); a per-test sign-in would make each test standalone.

- Done in H4-3 (ADR 0034): the server serves the diff (`GET …/pulls/:number/diff`, read from

  approvals" and "n comments" on real PRs.

- An owner named like a server prefix (`api`, `auth`, `assets`, `setup`, `webhooks`, `healthz`)
  gets the server's 404 at `/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>` (the SPA fallback skips those prefixes).
- Logins outside the display rule (`[A-Za-z0-9-]{1,39}`, e.g. Enterprise Managed Users with
  `_`) or a 64-hex head SHA fail the whole page (`parseShellData`), as they would the inbox;
  widen the rules or blank the field server-side.
- N5 (P6-6) still stands: the shell reads `GET …/review` once per load for the status pill even
  though the server answer now carries `myReview`; drop it once the fixture source carries one.
- LCP: add the cold-load measurement (P6-8 follow-up); the warm run now answers the PR request.
- The 409 (no active org) and 403 (not a member) answers show "could not be loaded"; an org
  picker would say more.
- Review round 1 (fixed): S1 request lines log the route pattern or `(unmatched)`, never the raw
  path (repository names); S2 the fixture switches work only in dev and `VITE_ROCKY_FIXTURES=on`
  builds, and production carries no fixture (initial JS 166.1 → 155.4 KB gz).
- Review round 1 nits (not done):
  - Timing oracle within an org: an unknown PR answers 404 before the GitHub access check, a known
    one after it, so response time tells a member which PR numbers exist in repositories they
    cannot read (same org only; numbers, not content).
  - The 503 test shows the answer, not that nothing was read from the database (the code checks
    first; a spy on `withOrg` would prove it).
  - `NOT_SPA` owner names differ between dev and production: an owner such as `setup`,
    `assets` or `healthz` gets the app from Vite in development but the server's fixed 404 in
    production (only `api` and `auth` are proxied in development).
  - Old `/?repo=<owner>/<name>&pr=<n>` links (from the inbox before H4-2) now open a fixture in
    development and `/inbox` in production; a redirect to `/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>` would keep them.
  - `vite.config.ts` resolves the proxy target on every command; resolve it only for `serve`, so a
    bad `ROCKY_DEV_SERVER_URL` cannot fail a build.
  - `localhost` is in the dev proxy's loopback list although the runbook asks for `127.0.0.1`
    (cookies are per host name); consider dropping it.
  - An author login outside the display rule fails the whole page; the server could answer
    `author: null` for one instead.
  - Runbook: create the GitHub App (runbook H3) before switching `ROCKY_PUBLIC_URL` to the Vite
    origin, or the manifest's URLs point at 5173 without a proxy for `/setup`.
  - `pull-request-page.ts`: tiebreak orders are inconsistent (`x.id desc` for the head run,
    `x.id` ascending for the document, as `reviews.ts`; `r.updated_at desc, r.id` for two repos of
    the same name); make them one documented rule.
- CI flake (not H4-2): `apps/worker/src/analysis-lane.e2e.test.ts` "slots 4, org cap 2, 1 finishing
  per step: B is never starved" failed once in CI on PR #43 ("expected 6 to be less than or equal
  to 5") and passed on re-run.
- e2e flake (P6-4 test, seen once in the H4-2 fix round, 25/25 on repeat):
  `drafts.e2e.spec.ts` "dismiss with a reason → refresh…" reads `reviewApi.decision("F1")`
  right after the optimistic card shows "dismissed", before the POST has landed; use
  `expect.poll`.
- ui-verifier nits: status pages have no brand mark, heading or per-state `document.title`; the
  503 pages offer no Retry; at 375 px the top bar's "by @author" is hidden (`hide-sm`), so phones
  show no author. Fixed: status-page links use `--you`, messages are centred.

  sticky footer; a contract test runs the real routes' JSON through the web parsers.
- N5: the shell reads `GET …/review` once per page load for the status pill (one extra request);
  drop it once the server serves the shell data with `myReview`.
- Done in H4-2 (real PR addresses read the server): N8: the shell data still comes from the fixture source, so against a real server Finish review
  404s (the fixture pull request id is not a row): a prerequisite for H4 is serving the shell
  data (`pullRequestId`, `analysisId`, `myReview`) from the server.

### P6-4 follow-ups

- **Done (H4-1, ADR 0033):** wire the repository access check in `startServer`; the drafts routes answer per access on a running server.
- Server-backed shell data with the real `analysisId` (P6-2/P6-3 follow-ups); until then the dev page's fixture ids are unknown to the server, so drafts fail to load in `pnpm dev` (the e2e suite answers the API itself). A Vite dev proxy for `/api` and `/auth` is also still missing.
- A new push starts a fresh set of decisions (they belong to the analysis): carry decisions and pending comments over to the next analysis when the finding and its lines still match, or show them as outdated.
- "Send to agent" only copies the fix prompt; a real hand-off needs an agent integration.
- P8: learn from `findings_state.reason` (dismissals) and accepted drafts; the panel's note then can say "will remember".
- **Done (P3-5 integration):** Merging with P3-5 (`loadReviewPlan` in `packages/db/src/reviews.ts`), whichever lands second: renumber its migration to 0016 and reconcile `schema.ts`; then in `ownComments` add `c.source = 'own'` (accepted drafts also have a `comments_pending` row, `source` `ai`/`ai_edited`, and would otherwise be offered twice) and filter on `author_id` and on the `analysis_id` the plan posts from; in `acceptedDrafts` filter on `f.member_id` (equal to `decided_by` on accepted rows; undo clears `decided_by`) and join `comments_pending` on (`org_id`, `analysis_id`, `finding_id`, `author_id = f.member_id`) and render with core `composePostedComment(finding, row)` instead of `findingCommentBody` (same format; the accepted card shows its parts), so an edited body is posted. The stored location (`path`, `line` = range end, `start_line` only for multi-line) is the same as the one P3-5 derives from the document; the `findings_state.id` of a draft stays the same across undo and re-accept. Decisions belong to the analysis the reviewer saw; P3-5 posts from the head revision's latest done analysis, so decisions on an older one are not posted (see the carry-over follow-up above).
- Toast at 375 px sits over the bottom of the open Review drawer for its 4 s.
- An edit replaces a draft's body only: the finding's title and suggestion are always posted with it (`composePostedComment`). Removing or changing the suggestion needs its own control.
- The e2e and visual suites answer the drafts API from a test-side fake (`apps/web/e2e/review-api.ts`), not the real server; the server's routes and persistence are tested on Postgres (`routes.db.test.ts`, `drafts.test.ts`). An e2e run against the real server needs server-backed shell data first.
- The copy "Rocky keeps your reason so future reviews can learn from it" (dismiss) and the panel's dismissal note promise P8 learning; reword them if P8 slips.
- Own comments are taken only on the analysed diff: on a newer push the composer is off and comments are hidden until that push is analysed (client-side; the server checks the line against the analysis, not the diff head).
- Keys a/e/d/n/p and their hints on the buttons are P6-5.
- Viewed state is still in memory only (P6-1 follow-up).
- `DiffBody` now renders its virtual list as one flat keyed list (rows kept their element when runs merged or split, which dropped the focus); worth a perf re-run on the 5,000-line fixture in CI.
- Fixed a time bomb in `packages/db/src/sessions.test.ts` (a session created at a fixed past date had expired by the database clock, so the "cannot delete a live session" check failed on any date after 2026-10-28).

  §3):** (1) read the live PR from GitHub right before submitting and answer `head_moved` without

- `pnpm test:e2e` now has a `perf` project on a production build; in CI it would need the same pinned-browser setup as the other e2e projects (P6-1 follow-up), and the 60 fps threshold depends on the runner's CPU.

- Screen-reader text: deleted lines read "removed: …" but hot-line severity has no text (the marker is visual only); add it with the drafts (P6-4).
- Both Playwright web servers (dev and production build) start on every run, even for one project; split the configs or make the build server conditional if it gets slow.
- Move the pr-847 diff out of `@rocky-reviews/evals` into a shared fixture (schema fixtures or a small fixtures package) so the web app does not depend on evals.
- When the analysis head is stale, the diff says the AI notes are hidden, but the AI strip ("What changed", "Why …") still shows; add an "earlier push" note there too.

Map a node to its file through `graph.nodes[].files`.

Pass only open findings to leave dismissed drafts off the badges. Retry button and reason text: `status: "unavailable"`, `reason`, `UNAVAILABLE_REASON_TEXT`.
- Layout: a label that needs room widens its corridor, so renaming an edge label can move later lanes sideways; edges into the same corridor from a fan-out bundle tightly; a node's `badges` (schema chips) are only in the tooltip, there is no room on the POC card. Flows, views and walkthrough are not drawn (not in P5).

Open: a wide fan-out still bundles its curves in one gutter; in deep chains a curve spanning many rows puts its label beside an unrelated card (labels sit at the curve's middle); the hero label pill can hide most of a short hero line; badge numbers on `--high`/`--med` (light) are 3.6:1 and unchanged edges (`--faint`) 2.3:1, POC tokens (P0-10 owner to decide); cards without a subtitle keep the two-line height.

### P3-5 follow-ups

- **Migration number:** renumbered to `0016_review_submissions` when rebased onto P6-4 (`0015_review_drafts`).
- **Needs human (product):** drafts in the plan are the member's own decisions (`decided_by` = the poster); comments without a line are not posted; a `post_failed` answer is final for its idempotency key. Veto at review.
- **Done (H4-1, ADR 0033):** the repository access check is built in `startServer`; `GET`/`POST …/review` answer per access.
- P6-6: render `GET …/review` as the exact list; send `contentSha256`, `headSha` and a fresh `idempotencyKey` per click; reload on `stale`/`head_moved`; show `post_failed` reasons; `post_unknown` means "check GitHub, the attempt is being reconciled".
- **Integrated with P6-4 (ADR 0027, ADR 0026 P15):** `loadReviewPlan` reads P6-4's per-reviewer rows: accepted drafts are `findings_state` (`member_id` = `decided_by` = the member) joined with their `ai`/`ai_edited` pending row and posted as core `composePostedComment` (what the accepted card shows; `findingCommentBody` removed); own comments are `comments_pending` with `source = 'own'`, the member's `author_id` and the plan's `analysis_id`, so an accepted draft is posted once. Tests: `packages/db/src/reviews.test.ts` "posts an accepted draft once…", "an edited draft posts the edited body…", "offers only own comments on the analysis it posts from…"; `apps/server/src/reviews/post-review.db.test.ts` "an accepted draft is posted once… (P6-4)" (through P6-4's draft routes, GitHub receives `composePostedComment` of the card's body). The posting routes moved to `apps/server/src/reviews/post-routes.ts` (P6-4 owns `routes.ts`).
- P6-7's `PullRequestApprover` port is not implemented yet: build it later on top of this poster (live-head check against GitHub right before submitting, idempotent per member/PR/head, ADR 0029 §3); until then bulk approval answers 503.
- **Security-boundary review fixes:** S1: the plan excludes findings carried (posting/posted) by the member from any analysis of the same revision, so a re-run does not post a finding twice (ADR 0026 P16; a re-run's drop-out of unposted decisions is a named residual risk). S2: `decideDraft` and `deleteOwnComment` take the posting lock (`lockReviewPlan`) and refuse a carried item with `already_posted` (409; a decision carries the current state, which P6-4's client already shows as a conflict) (P17). Nit 3: accepting or editing a draft whose composed comment would exceed 65,536 characters answers 400 `comment_too_long`. Nit 5: ESLint keeps `apps/worker` and `packages/llm` off any `server/src` path and `@rocky-reviews/server`, static or dynamic (`import()` selector), and off the `createGitHubReviewPoster` identifier; the source scan also forbids `server/src`.
- **Review fixes for #39:** SF1: carried drafts match by content (path, line, start line, sha256 of the posted markdown) instead of finding id, which is positional and renumbered by a re-run (`carriedDraftContent` in `packages/db/src/reviews.ts`, used by the plan and by `decideDraft` on accept/edit); the exact carried row still refuses undo/edit/dismiss. SF2: send to agent is never refused as `already_posted`. N3: the worker/LLM ESLint block also bans computed `import()` sources and `createRequire`. N4: tests pin member scoping, revision scoping and both lock orders. N1: late `posted` after reconcile named in ADR 0026 residual risks.
- P6-6 (N2, left for the Finish review work): the web client treats a 409 `already_posted` on delete and a 400 `comment_too_long` on accept/edit as generic failures (`saveFailed`/`deleteFailed` toasts); give them their own messages ("Already posted to GitHub", "Too long to post as one comment"), and show a 409 `already_posted` decision distinctly from a conflict.
- P6-6: show items carried by a `posting`/`posted` attempt as posted and read-only; the drafts routes answer 409 `already_posted` for them. The preview must render the AI bodies exactly as GitHub will (markdown, mentions, links, images; N7).
- Nit 6 (not shared): the plan picks the head revision's latest **done** analysis (`reviews.ts` `latestDoneAnalysis`), while the inbox (`packages/db/src/review-inbox.ts` lateral join) picks the latest analysis of **any** status, head first, to show its state. Different questions, so no shared selector; if the PR page ever shows a done analysis other than the latest done one of the head, align it with `latestDoneAnalysis` so the cards and the plan agree.
- Pre-validate comment lines against the revision's hunks before posting (today a line outside the diff is GitHub's 422, `rejected`).
- Review round 1 applied (S1–S4, N1–N7): reconciliation matches id/head/state/body digest/time margin/comment count and stays `posting` when ambiguous; a full 10-page listing throws (fail closed); ages by the database clock; a late `posted` overrides `lost`; `GET …/review` returns `inFlight` and `latestSubmission`; no DELETE on the attempt tables; ESLint keeps the poster out of `apps/worker` and `packages/llm`; a non-`ForgeReadError` from the poster is a definite `failed`; poster timeout 20 s.
- Product decisions to veto (N3): comments on an earlier revision drop out of the plan after a push; a `post_failed` answer is final for its key.
- N4: wiring `repositoryAccess` in `startServer` is a prerequisite for P6-6 and the H4 gate (the routes answer 503 until then). **Done (H4-1, ADR 0033).**
- N7: P6-6 must render the exact markdown GitHub will show for each item (mentions, links, images in LLM text are posted under the person's name).
- Reconciliation reads up to 1000 of the person's reviews on the pull request and fails closed beyond that; a pull request with more is an operator case.
- Revoke nothing, store nothing new: the refresh-token question (P7-1 follow-up) is unchanged; posting uses the same 8 h token as the access check.

### P7-2 follow-ups

- **Needs human:** the GitHub App must subscribe to the `organization` event (app settings → Permissions & events → Subscribe to events → Organization; the manifest now asks for it, existing apps need it ticked) and installations may need to accept it; without it people who leave the GitHub org keep their session until it expires (≤ 8 h).
- **Needs human (product):** review the role defaults in ADR 0024 (permission table, first owner = first GitHub org admin, GitHub role changes not mirrored, billing managers refused, a re-added member returns as `member`, an admin who leaves the GitHub org returns as `member`, admins can remove and restore other admins (remove + restore demotes an admin to member)); veto at review.
- **Done (H4-1, ADR 0033):** the repository access check is built in `startServer` (the app's key on the server) and passed as `AppDeps.repositoryAccess`, its `invalidateMember` as `AuthDeps.onMemberTokenChanged`.
- An owner who leaves the GitHub org stays the owner and cannot sign in: add a recovery path (runbook or a re-check that hands ownership to a GitHub org admin).
- Paging for `GET /api/org/members` (capped at 1000); a web UI for members and roles.
- Team `membership` events are ignored by design; if per-team access matters beyond P3-6's 5-minute bound, broadcast `invalidateMember` across instances (ADR 0011 follow-up) and from the worker.
- Test the 0014 down migration with owner rows and pending `org_member` inbox rows (and their `app.webhook_queue` lane rows) present: owners become admins, the inbox and queue rows are gone, and `up` applies again.

### P7-3 follow-ups

- **Needs human:** on the first Fly.io deploy, set `ROCKY_TRUST_PROXY` (`fly` direct, `xff:2` behind Cloudflare) and confirm with one request that the address Rocky counts is the client's (Fly appends to `X-Forwarded-For` and sets `Fly-Client-IP`; Cloudflare's chain); a wrong choice either lets clients spoof addresses or limits everyone as one.
- **Needs human:** add the CI job `zap baseline` to the required checks (`scripts/github/protect-main.sh`) once it has run green on GitHub's runners (its Linux path, `--network host`, was not run locally).
- Multi-instance: a Postgres-backed `RateLimitStore` (HMAC'd keys; share the HMAC secret, e.g. derived from `ROCKY_SESSION_SECRET`) before running more than one server machine; today each instance counts alone and a restart resets the counters (ADR 0025 R1).
- P9-3: call `app.rateLimits.consume(request, reply, "ask", memberId)` before any model call (and consider a per-org scope for metering); keep `ask` in `RATE_LIMIT_SCOPES`.
- P10-1: copy `apps/web/dist` into the image and set `ROCKY_WEB_DIR`; the ZAP scan of the image over https (HSTS, Secure cookies) belongs to L-4's staging scan.
- The setup routes (P3-1) still use `@fastify/rate-limit`; move them to `RateLimits` so there is one limiter (and drop the dependency).
- Webhooks: per-address limits do not stop distributed floods (edge, ADR 0025 R2). Since review round 1 `webhook.ip` counts rejections only (a penalty box), split by the own-App claim; set `GITHUB_APP_ID` wherever webhooks are on so the split works (without it every delivery is "other").
- Review round 1 follow-ups:
  - Shared-office NAT: `auth.login` and `auth.callback` count per address (no session yet), so a large office behind one address shares 30 starts per 10 minutes; find a better key (e.g. the login cookie for the callback) or document raising `ROCKY_RATE_LIMIT_AUTH_LOGIN`/`_CALLBACK` (ADR 0025 R8).
  - `scripts/security/semgrep.sh`: `--exclude artifacts` (and the other excludes) are unanchored, so any directory of that name anywhere is skipped; anchor them to the repository root.
  - CI `zap baseline` job: upload `artifacts/zap/` as a workflow artifact when it fails, so a High/Medium can be read without rerunning locally.
  - ZAP does not reach `/setup/github-app` (only registered with `GITHUB_APP_SETUP=true` and no app configured); scan it in a second run with that configuration.
  - `scripts/security/zap-baseline.sh` uses `chmod 777` on `artifacts/zap/` for the ZAP container's user; run the container with `--user "$(id -u):$(id -g)"` (or a group-writable directory) instead.
  - GitHub avatars (P7-2/P9 UI) will be blocked by `img-src 'self'` and COEP `require-corp`; serve them through a same-origin proxy rather than widening the CSP.
  - A route can set its own, weaker CSP and the hook keeps it; add a test (or a Semgrep rule) that allow-lists the routes allowed to set `content-security-policy` themselves (today: auth pages, setup pages via `pageCsp`).
  - `ROCKY_RATE_LIMIT_*` accepts maxima up to 9,999,999; cap them at a sane bound per scope.
- The Vite dev server does not send these headers (ADR 0025 R5); a `preview` configuration with the same CSP would let `pnpm test:e2e` run under it.
- `apps/web/src/build.test.ts` repeats the parse5 walk of `findInlineContent` (`apps/server`); move it to a small shared package if a third user appears. Markup the check does not refuse but the CSP blocks at runtime: `<form action>` to another origin (`form-action 'self'`), `<link rel=icon>` or `<a href>` to other origins (allowed by design), `<meta name=referrer>`; `<svg><use href>`, `<image>` and `<foreignObject>` inside SVG files served as images run nothing (CSP applies to them too).

Set `ROCKY_SESSION_SECRET` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) and `ROCKY_KEK_KEYS` in production secrets.

- **Needs human (review round 1):** the existing GitHub App installation(s) must accept the new organization permission **Members: read** (GitHub asks the org owner after the app's permissions change); until then the membership check fails and sign-in shows "No Rocky organization" for organization accounts. Also turn on "Expire user authorization tokens" in the app's settings: production refuses non-expiring tokens.
- P7-2 (review round 1): re-adding a member removed in Rocky (`members.removed_at`) is an explicit admin action, not a side effect of sign-in; org-wide routes (settings, members, billing) must gate on role, not just on the session; access for guests and outside collaborators is a later product decision; give `activeMember` a discriminated result (no session → 401, no active org or not a member → 403) instead of `null`; keep every GET free of side effects (a forced top-level GET is only harmless while that holds); a future bearer-token API needs its own `csrfExempt` kind, never `hmac_signature`.
- P7-3 (done in P7-3, ADR 0025): rate limits through `AuthDeps.rateLimit` on the auth scope are **required before production** (login starts are unauthenticated; `createLoginAttempt` prunes 100 expired rows per call, which bounds growth but not request volume); security headers for `/api/*` JSON answers. The auth HTML pages set their own CSP and `X-Frame-Options`: do not duplicate or loosen them.
- Notes (review round 1, not fixed): a logout racing a login of the same user in another browser can clear the token the new session just stored (availability only: the next request that needs the token asks the person to sign in again); `view()` opens one transaction per org, batch them if people have many orgs; `created_at <= now` at lookup assumes server clocks within the touch throttle of each other, so clock skew across instances can make a just-created session look not yet valid on another instance (fails closed).
- Revoke the token at GitHub on logout (`DELETE /applications/{client_id}/token`); a periodic `sessions.purgeEnded` job (today nothing calls it); session-secret versioning for rotation without invalidating CSRF tokens.
- P7-2 (review round 2): someone who leaves the GitHub org keeps that org in their live sessions until they expire (at most the token lifetime, about 8 h; GitHub already refuses the token for the org's private repos). Handle the `organization` member_removed webhook (or re-check membership on use): clear their token, cut the org from live sessions and call `changed()`. On a `not_a_member` sign-in for an org where a member row exists, clear its token too.

- P3-5: if sessions must outlive the 8 h token, store the refresh token (encrypted) and serialise refreshes across the member rows of every org.

### P4-8 follow-ups

- Needs human: the first live run on a `rocky-reviews/rocky` PR with a provider key (`docs/cli.md`, "First live run"). It is the criterion "runs on a rocky-reviews/rocky PR" against real GitHub and a real model; the tests prove it on the real forge adapter against a fake GitHub and a scripted model. Check stage schemas on structured outputs (ADR 0014 R6) and tune `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and the default cap from the reported usage.
- P4-5: pass a `UsagePricer` (`apps/worker/src/cli/render.ts`) built from the price table, so the usage block prints cost per role and in total; price `attempts` by the provider's refusal billing rules, as metering does.
- P4-6: once token limits exist, have `--dry-run` use them (per-file split, graph budget) instead of its own estimate, and let the cap check use a real token count where the provider offers one.
- The cap is a best-effort stop before the next call (review round 1): one call can report more than its reservation (provider prompt overhead, a fallback chain longer than the one attempt assumed), and a call that throws (timeout, network) after the provider started billing reports no usage, so the cap cannot count it.
- When the cap refuses a call, the pipeline aborts the sibling calls in flight; whatever the provider billed for them before the abort is not reported, so the printed usage undercounts.
- `--dry-run --json` silently ignores `--json` (the plan is text only); either print the plan as JSON or refuse the combination.
- The PR-number argument accepts up to 10 digits, more than the schema's `MAX_PR_NUMBER`: such a number reads GitHub and then fails as `invalid_request`. Bound it in `parseCliArgs`.
- No tests for `apps/worker/src/rocky.ts` (env wiring, exit codes) or direct tests for `cli/plan.ts` (only through the dry-run test).
- `STAGE_CALLS` (core) should be the single source the pipeline itself uses for each stage's role, instructions, output and cap; today the pipeline names them at each call and `stages.test.ts` checks they agree.

Neither changes what a tenant pays at list price (P11 recomputes from tokens), but consider `GENERATED ALWAYS` without the override grant and an upper-bound CHECK.
- Fallback model ids come from the provider (a server-side `fallback` attempt names the model that ran). Today they only need to match the model shape; restrict them (no `@`) or map them to the price catalogue so a provider cannot pick an id that later matches a different price entry.

- The `usageTargets` guard in `createAnalysisRunner` checks presence only (shallow); validate every role has a target.

- Review round 1 notes:
  - N2: keep a local lease deadline in the lane (claim time + lease, moved only by renewals that succeeded) and abort a run past it, so a renewal outage longer than the lease cannot let two workers spend on one row (ADR 0017 R3).
  - N3: `finish()` checks `status` and `lease_id` but not `lease_until > now()`, unlike `loadAnalysisTarget` and `renewAnalysisLease`; a write just after expiry but before a reclaim still lands. Harmless today (no one else holds the row), but make the three consistent.
  - N4: ADR 0017 R2 wording fixed (the overrun is bounded by the files counted as reused).
  - N6: the lane registers a run in its slot map only after `deps.run` has been called synchronously; take the slot before starting the run so a throwing `run` can never leave the counts off.
  - N7: migration 0012 creates the partial unique index without first collapsing duplicate live rows of one revision. None can exist in a deployment (nothing enqueued before 0012), but a dev database with planted rows would fail the migration; dedupe first if that ever happens.
  - Dev databases that applied the `0011_reserved_p4_5` placeholder get a checksum mismatch once it is replaced by P4-5's real 0011: run `pnpm db:migrate down 2` before rebasing (or recreate the dev database).
- Fairness-test flake fix (2026-10-30, review notes, not fixed):
  - The "B's forge reads last" reproduction relies on a 50 ms timer in the fake forge; a deterministic hold (B's `openRepository` waits until A's have run) would not depend on timing.
  - `steppedForge`'s `current.getStore() ?? newGate()` fallback is unused by the lane path; it could throw instead, so a run outside `claimed` cannot silently fall back to forge-call order.
  - Gates that are never reached (a run that ends before its diff read) are never removed from `gates`.
  - Pre-existing, cause unknown: load cases with cap 1 and `finish: 1` (not in the suite) end the step loop early with `expected length 50 but got 3/4`.

- **R1 decided (user, 2026-10-27):** in-file suppression is accepted and labelled; criterion 1 is the deterministic floor plus "unchanged unless a payload reached a risky file". Gap found in round 1: a payload inside a risky file that no rule floors (pr-912's `src/billing/charge.ts`) can steer that file's own triage to low, so it is never reviewed and there is nothing to label; the verdict falls from high to low. Closing it needs a deterministic risk signal for such paths (more path rules such as `billing`/`payment`, or content rules such as payment SDK imports). Needs a decision on how wide the rules may go.
- P6 (renderer gate, from `/security-review`): the schema 1.1 markup rule (I12) is a backstop, not the control. Any task that renders model text as markdown must disable raw HTML (`<img src>`, `<a href>` pass the rule), check link and image targets after entity decoding (`[x](&#x68;ttps://…)` passes), and turn off bare-URL autolinking. That task gets its own security review.
- P4-7 (done, ADR 0018): `pnpm evals run --injection` runs `INJECTION_CORPUS` on both fixtures with the `check.ts` checks and reports verdict changes, floors, findings outside the diff, leaks and poisoned drafts per case. Still open: paraphrase, translation and encoding leak probes (R3).

- P9 (ask), P9-3: reuse the corpus, `completeWithRepair` and the schema text rules so answers get the leak guard; PR bodies and comments become new channels and need corpus cases.
- P4-8: dogfooding trips the canary on PRs that touch `prompt.ts`, ADR 0015 or the corpus tests (R6); expect those files unanalysed.
- Self-host docs: recommend servers that do not parse special tokens in message content (R5).
- Review round 1 nits:
  - Break chat-token delimiters (`<|`, `|>`, `<｜`) rather than whole tokens, which also covers tokens not on the list; fill the gaps (e.g. `<|fim_*|>` variants already match, `<think>` does not).
  - The final check's allowed text is every file's path and patch; it should be only the files actually sent to a model (noise and too-large files are not).
  - The canary is public, so a PR author can plant it to make a unit fail on purpose; the fallback briefing and the floors now limit what that buys (a unanalysed file raises the verdict), but a canary that only the final check sees still fails the analysis (R4).

- P4-6 (queue): the analysis lane. - Content-based generated-file detection (ADR 0014 R1); per-org rule overrides.

- Non-UTF-8 diffs fail the whole analysis (`diff_not_utf8`); decode per file and mark only the affected file unanalysed.

### P3-3 fix: webhook queue trigger plan (migration 0009)

`worker.webhook_queue_on_update()` joins the statement's OLD and NEW transition tables. plpgsql
plans that join once per connection and reuses the plan; the first firing is usually a 1-row
worker update, so the cached plan was a nested loop, quadratic for every later bulk update on that
connection (a 20k-row UPDATE took ~49 s; `inbox.test.ts` timed out in CI on #15). Migration 0009
sets `enable_nestloop = off` on that one function, which leaves hash or merge join; the trigger's
result is unchanged. Tests: `inbox.test.ts` "a bulk update stays linear on a connection whose
trigger plan was made for one row (0009)" warms the plan with a 1-row update on one client, then
bounds a 20k-row update at 10 s (49 s without 0009, 0.6 s with it), and checks the function's
setting; "a 20k-row backlog…" also bounds its cleanup update. Lesson for transition-table
triggers: the plan is cached per connection, so choose a join strategy that is safe at any size.

Approved with these items open (no code in this task):

- A separate signing job would narrow `id-token: write` to the step that needs it, instead of the whole `images` job.
- `apps/worker/src/heartbeat.ts` keeps the mode of a heartbeat file that already existed (it sets 0600 on creation only); `fchmod` after open, or refuse a pre-existing file.
- The builder fetches pnpm with `npm install -g pnpm@<version>` (version-pinned, no integrity hash); use corepack with `COREPACK_INTEGRITY_KEYS`, or pin the tarball hash, once corepack's future in Node 24 is settled.
- `scripts/security/trivy.sh` does `chmod 777` on `artifacts/images` so the root Trivy container can write; run the container with `--user` and a matching owner instead.
- Integration with PR #81 (P10-3): `docs/self-host/configuration.md` and its completeness test must get rows for `ROCKY_HEARTBEAT_FILE`, `ROCKY_DB_APP_USER`, `ROCKY_DB_APP_PASSWORD`, `ROCKY_DB_WORKER_USER`, `ROCKY_DB_WORKER_PASSWORD` (all in `.env.example` here); whichever lands second adds them.
- `deploy/rocky.env` is one file for both the server and the worker; splitting it would keep the LLM keys away from the server. `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` is a `\n`-escaped line in that file; file-based secrets (`*_FILE`, compose `secrets:`) need app support.

### P2-3 / P2-4 final-review follow-ups

Approved with these minor items open:

- `packages/log`: a log call can throw on an Error whose `message` is not a string or whose getter throws, and on a revoked or trapping Proxy (`scrub.ts` `walkError`, `isPlainObject`). Read those inside `try` and coerce with `String()`.
- `packages/log`: objects and Maps with ~1M entries are fully read before the node budget applies (`readEntries`, `[...map]`). Stop after `MAX_NODES`.
- `packages/log`: prefixed error codes such as `FST_ERR_*` are redacted; accept `^[A-Z]{2,10}_ERR_[A-Z0-9_]+$`.
- `packages/crypto`: key material and plaintext come from Node's shared Buffer pool (`Buffer.concat`, `Buffer.from`). Copy into `Buffer.alloc` (unpooled).
- `packages/crypto`: AAD fields with lone surrogates encode to the same bytes as U+FFFD. Reject `!isWellFormed()` with `invalid_context`.
- Nits: key names `passwordHash`, `pat`, `bearer`; `msgPrefix` not scrubbed; non-object `child()` bindings; `AggregateError.errors` dropped; string plaintext temp buffer in `keyring.encrypt` not zeroed; document that `rewrapDataKeys` skips keys already on the current KEK.

### P2-1 / P2-2 / P2-5 / P2-6 final-review follow-ups

Approved with these minor items open:

- The provisioning check in migration 0001 covers only plpgsql and sql and runs once. Check every trusted language (`pg_language where lanpltrusted`), and repeat the checks on every `migrate()` run or at server start (catches drift, restores, and direct grants to the login role).
- Plain `SET` (e.g. `search_path`, `statement_timeout`) inside `withOrg` persists on the pooled connection for the next tenant. It can't expose data (the app can't create objects), but it can break queries. Run `RESET ALL` before `begin`, or refuse `SET` that isn't `SET LOCAL`.
- Nits: revoke the `lo_*` large-object functions from PUBLIC (global storage outside tenancy); use `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` on the SECURITY DEFINER functions.

### P2-3 dump-test review follow-ups

Approved with these minor items open:

- `findKeyMaterial` (`packages/crypto/src/testing.ts`) has no octal `bytea_output = escape` form for key bytes, so a raw key in a future `bytea` column dumped in escape format would not be found. Current secret columns reject raw bytes.
- `replaceOrgDataKey` checks only the version: require `version === expected` (re-wrap) or `expected + 1` (rotation), and ideally compare the stored wrapped bytes.
- Add a concurrency test for the insert-once and compare-and-swap writes (two `withOrg` in `Promise.all`, exactly one succeeds).

## Remote verification (2026-10-25, first push to rocky-reviews/rocky)

- First CI run on `main`: verify, CodeQL, Semgrep, gitleaks, osv-scanner all green; `verify` took 37 s (P0-1: under 5 min).
- `scripts/github/protect-main.sh` applied: PR required, 7 required checks pinned to the GitHub Actions app, linear history, signed commits, no force push, enforced for admins; secret scanning + push protection on (P0-3).
- `cla-signatures` branch created (P0-2).
- Pre-commit gitleaks inside worktrees (the containerised scan could not resolve the worktree's `.git` file and scanned 0 commits; found while committing P2-1) is fixed on `main` by PR #5. The P2 commits were scanned by hand as patches with the pinned image: no leaks.
- This PR is the P0-4 dry run: opened from a task branch with the PR template, merged only after all required checks passed.
